Overrides the Command toggle (user decision). Companions pulled off jobs must be reassigned (user accepted).

Previous version: **1.4.56** — build tag `v74-sleep-rules-diag`. Verified: branches 0/1686, maxstack 0/1686, check_refs 0, ResolveRefs 16950/16950, JIT 1631/1631, version 1.4.56. Untested in-game.
- User log ceb1767e (1.4.55): no errors. User turned on Repair (mode 15 = CompanionAI.UpdateRepairBuildingsMode, the original "repair buildings" job, zero logging) for a while; companion didn't follow (recalled 3x), then after Repair off it stood still next to the kiln for minutes (DoorHandler 'stuck' at a fixed pos) and wouldn't sleep. Sleep: TryAutoSleep required ActionMode==0 and !StayHome and EnvMan.IsNight (log ended right after evening music). The original mod's EverybodyIsTryingToSleep postfix blocked the night skip until EVERY mode-0 companion was asleep - no range/bed/timeout check ("Waiting for X to sleep...").
- **Sleep rules (user design):** new config section "Sleep": `SleepAtNight` (true), `SleepWhenPlayerSleeps` (true), `BedRange` (50, 10-200). TryAutoSleep: if SleepWhenPlayerSleeps && local player InBed -> go now (skips night check, scan timer and post-SleepStop suppression; clears `_mhrStayAwake`); else needs !_mhrStayAwake && SleepAtNight && IsNight. Job-mode and StayHome gates REMOVED. New gate: claimed bed within BedRange (`MHR_BedInRange`), else stays up. Logs "[Rest] Going to bed: ..." with the reason. UpdateSleeping's day-wake skipped while the local player is in bed (`MHR_LocalPlayerInBed`).
- **Work pause:** Harvest/Smelt/Farm/Fish/Cook/Repair(gear) controllers' Update starts with `CompanionRest.MHR_ShouldPauseWork(this)` (sleeping, or navigating to a bed) -> return. Homestead already aborts on IsResting; UpdateAI is gated by Resting / handles IsNavigating early, so repair-buildings/restock modes pause too. Job ActionMode stays set -> resumes in the morning. Risk to watch: a controller paused mid-task (e.g. holding a chest open) until morning.
- **Night skip:** `SleepPatches.MHR_ShouldWaitFor(rest)` replaces IsFollowMode in EverybodyIsTryingToSleep_Patch and UpdateSleeping_Patch: wait only if local player in bed < 30s (`s_mhrBedSince`) and `rest.MHR_WantsBedNow()` (not sleeping, ZDO owner, SleepWhenPlayerSleeps, bed in range).
- **Recall:** FireComeToMe (long press come-to-me; CancelAll already StopAll()s rest) now calls `CompanionRest.MHR_StayAwakeForRecall(setup)` -> `_mhrStayAwake = true` (no night auto-sleep) until the player is next in bed (TryAutoSleep player path clears it) or SleepStop (`MHR_ClearStayAwakeAll`).
- **Diagnostics:** `Companions.MHR_Diag` (C# `MHR_Diag.cs` compiled with stub Companions types against real UnityEngine.CoreModule/assembly_valheim/SoftReferenceableAssets + mono's Facades/netstandard, class body spliced; stubs dropped) ticked from MHR_RespawnTracker.Tick: for owned companions with Follow on and StayHome off, if >10m from the local player and moved <1m for 10s -> one `[Diag]` warning: name, time, distance, pos, jobMode, active controllers, followTarget (`CompanionAI.MHR_DiagFollowName`), foundPath (`MHR_DiagFoundPath`), inCombat, recoveringTombstone, rest sleeping/navigating/resting. Repair-buildings: `[RepairBuild] Heading to repair "<piece>" (Nm away)`, `Stopped working on a piece: <reason>` (AbortRepairBuild), `Finished repairing a piece`.
- Not changed: sitting at a fire still blocks TryAutoSleep (Update returns in the sitting branch) - original behavior.

Previous version: **1.4.55** — build tag `v73-stick-input-fallback`. Verified: branches 0/1666, maxstack 0/1666, check_refs 0, ResolveRefs 16871/16871, JIT 1612/1612, version 1.4.55. Untested in-game.
- Player report: `MissingMethodException: single ZInput.GetJoyRightStickX()` in CompanionRadialMenu.UpdateAnimation (earlier: GetJoyLeftStickX in Update) - their game build has a different ZInput stick signature than 1.0.16 (we build against Sept 25 1.0.16). User asked the reporter for game version / controller / input mods; built the fix regardless.
- New class `Companions.MHR_Stick` (C# source `MHR_Stick.cs` in work/, compiled with mcs against the stub BepInEx with a throwaway CompanionsPlugin stub, disassembled with `MONO_PATH=. monodis`, class body spliced to end of IL): `LX/LY/RX/RY()` resolve `ZInput` via `Type.GetType("ZInput, assembly_utils")` (fallback assembly_valheim), pick the `GetJoy*Stick*` overload returning float whose params are all bool/optional (fewest params; static preferred), use a `Func<float>` delegate for static no-arg, else MethodInfo.Invoke on null / ZInput instance (property `instance`, field `instance` or `m_instance`). Failures -> 0 + one `[Input]` warning. All 8 call sites (`[assembly_utils]ZInput::GetJoy*Stick*()`) now call MHR_Stick. Other ZInput calls (GetButtonDown etc.) are still direct.

Previous version: **1.4.54** — build tag `v72-new-internal-id`. Verified: branches 0/1656, maxstack 0/1656, check_refs 0, ResolveRefs 16844/16844 (real BepInEx), JIT 1603/1603, version 1.4.54 in BepInPlugin. Untested in-game.

Added `[BepInIncompatibility("com.profmags.companions")]` so Reborn is skipped if ProfMags' original is installed. **Version-bump checklist note: the BepInPlugin blob now starts `01 00 21 4D 61 64 64 68...` (GUID 33 bytes), name 0x19 bytes, then `06 31 2E 34 2E 35 34` for "1.4.54" - edit the version bytes at the end; regenerate with a script if lengths change.** monodis --customattr in verify_run fails to decode the incompatibility attribute (stub BepInEx) - harmless; ResolveRefs confirms it against the real one.
- Config: the 1.4.53 redirect (custom ConfigFile + reflection on `<Config>k__BackingField`) is removed. `MHR_GetConfigFile()` now runs `MHR_MigrateOldConfig()` (if `Config.ConfigFilePath` missing and com.profmags.companions.cfg exists -> File.Copy + `Config.Reload()`) in try/catch, then returns base.Config. Users who ran 1.4.53 already have the right file name.
- User reported F1 changes "sometimes" not applying (thought it was the torch opener) and F8 seemed OK this time; still testing. Torch settings are read live (ModConfig.UseTorchOpener etc. each use), and ModConfig.Bind adds every entry to ModConfig.Categories for the F8 panel.

Previous version: **1.4.53** — build tag `v71-companion-id-config-starter`. Verified: branches 0/1656, maxstack 0/1656, check_refs 0, **ResolveRefs 16850/16850 MemberRefs resolved against REAL BepInEx 5.4.23.2 + game DLLs**, JIT 1603/1603, BepInPlugin 1.4.53. Untested in-game.
- **New verification step: `ResolveRefs.exe`** (source ResolveRefs.cs, built in scratchpad/refcheck with verify_run DLLs + real BepInEx core DLLs from GitHub BepInEx_win_x64_5.4.23.2.zip). Resolves every MemberRef token (0x0A table) in Companions.dll via Module.ResolveMember -> catches missing methods/fields in ANY assembly (confirmed: flags the 1.4.50 CreateCompanionTombstone bug). The work/ BepInEx.dll and verify_run/BepInEx.dll are a 4.6KB STUB (stub_bepinex.cs) - JIT sweep can't catch BepInEx API mistakes; ResolveRefs can. **Pipeline: ilasm -> check_branches -> check_maxstack -> check_refs -> ResolveRefs (in refcheck/) -> verify_all (in verify_run/) -> BepInPlugin check.**
- **Companion ID:** `CompanionSetup.MHR_CompanionIdHash` ("HC_CompanionId", Guid "N" string) created by `MHR_EnsureCompanionId()` (owner only; called from Update until set, `_mhrHasId`). CreateCompanionTombstone stamps tomb `MHR_TombCompanionIdHash` ("HC_TombCompanionId"); MHR_ResurrectCore copies it to the revived companion (local V_50) right before the bed copy. `MHR_IsOwnGrave`: tomb with ID -> match only on equal IDs; tomb without ID (pre-1.4.53) -> legacy name + HC_TombOwner fallback (user's choice). Despawn graves carry the despawned ID -> nobody matches. ID is log-only (user's choice).
- **Beds by ID:** `CompanionRest.MHR_BedClaimIdHash` ("HC_BedClaimId") on the bed ZDO next to the name. `MHR_SetBedClaimName` replaced by `MHR_SetBedClaim(bed, name, id)` (writes when either differs); `MHR_GetBedClaimId`, `MHR_MyId()`, `MHR_BedIsMine(bed)` (ID if bed has one, else name); `MHR_BedClaimedByOther` = has claim && !mine; ReleaseClaimedBed clears only if mine; relink rejects beds claimed by another; FindUnclaimedBedNear returns own (by ID) bed immediately. Live-claim refresh (30s) keeps name/ID current -> rename updates label.
- **Config rename:** Awake calls `MHR_GetConfigFile()` -> try `MHR_OpenRebornConfig()` (Paths.ConfigPath/Maddhattr.OfflineCompanionsReborn.cfg; File.Copy from com.profmags.companions.cfg if new missing; `new ConfigFile(path, true, MetadataHelper.GetMetadata(this))`; reflection-set BaseUnityPlugin `<Config>k__BackingField` so F1 ConfigurationManager sees it) catch -> fall back to base.Config. GUID stays com.profmags.companions (protects against loading alongside the original; possible external GUID checks).
- **Starter window + Valheim Plus:** user's reporter found V+ "disable I have arrived message" skips Game.UpdateRespawn's first-spawn block, which also contains the `Game.m_playerInitialSpawn` invoke we subscribe to. Subscription now points to `CompanionManager.MHR_StarterOnce()` (once per Game instance via `s_mhrStarterGame`); `MHR_StarterFallbackTick()` (from MHR_RespawnTracker.Tick, 1/s) calls it 5s after the local player exists, is alive and !InIntro() if the event never came.

Current version: **1.4.50** — build tag `v68-wake-and-reequip-fix`. Verified: branches 0/1640, maxstack 0/1640, JIT 1587/1587, BepInPlugin 1.4.50. Untested in-game.
- User log 9314eb51 (1.4.49): grave pin stayed (good), no exceptions. Two issues: (1) `SleepStop_Patch` (Game.SleepStop postfix) wakes companions via CancelDirected, but `EnvMan.IsNight()` is still true for a few seconds after the skip, so TryAutoSleep walked it back into bed and it woke again at "day started". Each wake restores bed collisions while the companion overlaps the bed, which shoves it ~1m aside (the "moved to the side of the bed"). Fix: new `CompanionRest._mhrNoAutoSleepUntil` + `MHR_SuppressAutoSleep(float)`; SleepStop postfix suppresses auto-sleep 120s; TryAutoSleep returns early while suppressed. (2) After a respawn with kept gear, the first OnInventoryChanged ran AutoEquipBest with 1 item (chest queued); the deferred re-run hit AutoEquipBest's 1.4.37 guard (`_equipQueue.Count > 0` -> ret) and was dropped, so legs/helmet waited for a later inventory change. Fix: the guard now re-arms `_autoEquipPending = true` and `_autoEquipCooldown = 1` (the Update block only fires pending when the cooldown crosses zero, so the cooldown must be > 0).
- User tested ValheimPlus (the Nexus reporter's suspect for blocking starter spawn) on a fresh character and saw no issue.

Previous version: **1.4.49** — build tag `v67-grave-pin-empty-fix`. Verified: branches 0/1639, maxstack 0/1639, JIT 1586/1586, BepInPlugin 1.4.49. Untested in-game.
- User log ad4e83eb (1.4.48): bed relink + claim saved on bed worked, recovery worked ("Found its own tombstone 8m away with 12 item(s)" -> "Collected 12"), no exceptions from our mod. Found: with DeathItemRule=1 (keep all) the tombstone is empty, so MHR_SweepGravePins removed its pin within a second of death - bad when auto-respawn is off (the grave is the manual revive point). Fix: `MHR_HasFullGraveNear` also counts a grave whose `RespawnPrefabHash` is still set (companion not yet revived). New `CompanionSetup.MHR_TombItemsHash` ("HC_TombItems", int) written in CreateCompanionTombstone = items put in the grave; resurrect core drops the pin when V_23 is false OR HC_TombItems == 0 (far path can't see the container, so it used to always keep the pin). `[DIAG] Reached end of grid setup` LogWarning -> LogDebug. Known harmless warning: `InitSkillDefs failed` (see below).
- User confirmed the bed name on the bed ZDO is a label only (custom key "HC_BedClaimName"); vanilla Bed owner stays 0, so players can still claim/sleep/spawn there and a player claim wins.

Previous version: **1.4.48** — build tag `v66-bed-claim-grave-recovery`. Verified: branches 0/1639, maxstack 0/1639 (1 known unreachable: AutoEquipBest), JIT 1586/1586, BepInPlugin 1.4.48. Untested in-game.
- From user log 673f427f (1.4.47). **Bed label:** `ClaimBed` still wrote `_setup.name` ("HC_Companion(Clone)") - 1.4.44 only fixed the relink path. Label also lived only in the static `_bedClaimNames` dict keyed by instance ID (lost on death/zone reload). Now: `CompanionRest.MHR_BedClaimNameHash` ("HC_BedClaimName") string on the **bed's ZDO** (ClaimOwnership only when value changes) via `MHR_SetBedClaimName/MHR_GetBedClaimName`; `MHR_MyName()` = CompanionInteract.GetCompanionName. ClaimBed, relink, and live-claim refresh (TryClaimBedIfNeeded every 30s) write it; ReleaseClaimedBed clears it if it's ours; `FindUnclaimedBedNear` skips beds claimed by another name (`MHR_BedClaimedByOther`); hover patch reads bed ZDO first (skips if player owns bed), dict fallback. `GetBedOwnerSafe` made `assembly`. Side effect: a permanently-gone companion's bed keeps its label until claimed by someone else.
- **Passive / standing:** `IsRecoveringTombstone` was true whenever the companion ZDO had a TombstoneId, even with the grave 270m away -> UpdateCombat disengaged, UI-close skipped follow restore, until TombstoneNavTimeout (120s scanTotal) then ClearTombstoneId (items abandoned). Now: IsRecoveringTombstone = `_pendingTombstone != null` only; UpdateCombat's recovery disengage removed; scan phase matches ANY own grave via `CompanionAI.MHR_IsOwnGrave(tombZdo, myZdo, character)` (tomb has TombstoneIdHash, `ZDOVars.s_ownerName` == companion name, and new `CompanionSetup.MHR_TombOwnerHash` "HC_TombOwner" (owner player id, written in CreateCompanionTombstone) empty or equal to companion OwnerHash) with items, within TombstoneRecoveryRange; no give-up; pending phase returns false while `IsInCombat` (combat runs, recovery resumes); pending nav-timeout -> 60s `_tombRecoverSnooze`; `CancelTombstoneRecovery` (player commands / player opening the grave) -> 30s snooze. Logs `[Recovery] Found...`, `[Recovery] Collected...`, `[Recovery] Couldn't reach...`.
- First respawn in that log was naked because DeathItemRule 0 + normal world = drop all (player parity, user's choice). User confirmed the companion did loot the grave once they got close.
- **Grave map pins:** resurrect core no longer calls RemoveDeathPin(name); it calls `MHR_RemoveGravePinNear(tombZdo.GetPosition())` only when nothing remains (V_23 false). LootTombstone removes pin when it empties the grave. `MHR_SweepGravePins()` (called from MHR_RespawnTracker.Tick, 1/s): for each Death-type pin whose name ends with " "+Loc(hc_minimap_grave), within 30m of the local player and `ZNetScene.IsAreaReady(pin)`, remove it if `MHR_HasFullGraveNear` finds no companion tombstone with items within 8m. Position-based (8m) so multiple graves keep their own pins.
- verify_all.exe must be run from INSIDE verify_run (copy the exe there) - running `../verify_all.exe` makes Mono probe the work/ dir and fail to find UnityEngine.CoreModule.

Previous version: **1.4.47** — build tag `v65-respawn-location-fix`. Verified: branches 0/1628, maxstack 0/1628, JIT 1575/1575, BepInPlugin 1.4.47. Untested in-game.
- User log e3c90497 (1.4.46): tracker worked (tracked, fired on time: "Respawn time reached ... far away") then NRE in MHR_ResurrectCore at IL 0x1d7 = LOC_MANUAL's `comp.transform` (comp null). Root cause PRE-DATES Stage B: the 1.4.31 location-selection block (bed -> owner -> world spawn) was inserted after `IL_0178: ret` with no label, and `IL_0161: brfalse.s IL_0179` (prefab found) jumped straight to LOC_MANUAL. So since 1.4.31 EVERY auto-respawn used the tombstone position (explains earlier logs of companions resurrecting where they died). Fixed: label `MHR_LOC_SELECT` on the block, IL_0161 retargeted; LOC_MANUAL now falls to LOC_NOBED if comp is null; bed respawn uses `CompanionManager.FindSpawnPosition(bedPos, 2)` instead of the raw bed position.
- Also found: the core never copied the bed claim to the revived companion (claim lives on the companion ZDO as HasHomeBedHash/HomeBedPosHash, read by TryClaimBedIfNeeded relink). Every death lost the claim. Now copied from RespawnHasBedHash/RespawnBedPosHash. ("Bed says unoccupied" while the companion is dead is expected - the label dictionary is held by the living companion and relinked on load.)
- **check_maxstack.py now also reports UNREACHABLE code** (instructions the stack simulation never visits). First run found: `AutoEquipBest` (intentional - 1.4.26 bypass of highest-block-power shield pick; ignore) and `CompanionTalk.SayContextual` - the 1.4.30 "no bed" speech block was also inserted after a `ret` and skipped by `IL_0042: brfalse.s IL_0050`; fixed via label `MHR_NOBED_CHECK` (restores a documented feature; user told, can veto). Lesson (3rd time): code inserted after a `ret` needs a label AND the preceding branch retargeted - the unreachable check now catches it automatically.
- Nexus bug report (v1.4.40, "Error"): `MissingMethodException VisEquipment.SetHelmetItem(string)` from `HideArmorPatch.Postfix` - NOT our mod (no such class, no string SetXItem calls left since 1.4.29) and not original ProfMags code; a third-party mod not updated for the Sep 25 game patch. It throws inside `MonoUpdaters.Update`'s un-guarded VisEquipment step, which skips the rest of that frame's updaters (FootStep, InstanceRenderer, WaterTrigger, LightFlicker, SmokeSpawner, CraftingStation) - likely cause of the reporter's flickering campfires (Rested link plausible, unproven). Also from that report: Haldor purchase tab only injects into ProfMags' Trader Overhaul UI (`TraderOverhaul.TraderUI`) - no vanilla store fallback; README currently still says companions can be recruited from Haldor. Starter companion hooks `Game.m_playerInitialSpawn` (first spawn in a world only). Offered to user: README wording, starter-on-existing-worlds, vanilla Haldor purchase - pending decision.

Previous version: **1.4.46** — tombstone/respawn **Stage B** + wider stance zone. Build tag `v64-respawn-tracker-stageB`. Verified: branches 0/1628, maxstack 0/1628, JIT 1575/1575, BepInPlugin 1.4.46. Untested in-game.
- **Stance zone** tuned from user log 3d762175 (logging from 1.4.44/45): intended stance pushes peaked 0.33-0.77 (every miss overshot the 0.65 edge by a little); intended outer pushes peaked 0.80-1.00 and crossed the zone in 0.02-0.08s. Now dead zone <0.25, stance 0.25-0.80, outer >=0.80. Hold time still 0.1s. Logging kept.
- **Stage B architecture:** `DoResurrect(bool)` is now a thin wrapper around new static `CompanionTombstoneResurrect.MHR_ResurrectCore(ZDO tombZdo, CompanionTombstoneResurrect comp, bool isAuto)` - the old body converted so it runs off the tombstone's ZDO; `comp == null` means the tombstone isn't loaded (far away). All 9 `this` uses patched (container lookup null-guarded; PlayResurrectEffects called with null `this` via `call` - it never touches `this`; items-remain branch destroys comp only if non-null, else sets ZDO bool `HC_TombCleanup`; nothing-remains branch returns if comp null). Before changing/removing the tombstone the core now does `tombZdo.SetOwner(ZDOMan.GetSessionID())` so ZNetScene.Destroy / field writes are authoritative (previously a non-owner game only destroyed it locally). Core calls `MHR_RespawnTracker.RemovePending(tombZdo.m_uid)` once the data is valid (manual or auto).
- Far path (comp null): no container visible -> sets V_23 (items may remain), wires companion `TombstoneIdHash` so it recovers them, marks tombstone `HC_TombCleanup`. `TombstoneAwakeResurrectPatch` now attaches the component in `_cleanupOnly` mode for flagged tombstones; its Update calls `MHR_DoCleanup()` (empty -> ClaimOwnership + ZNetScene.Destroy; items -> just remove the component, repeats each load until emptied). IMPORTANT: the IL_0018 label in that patch had to move onto the inserted code (the original brtrue.s targeted it) - watch for this when inserting after a `ret`.
- Respawn time: `WriteRespawnData` now writes long `HC_RespawnAt` = `ZNet.GetTimeSeconds() + AutoRespawnDelaySeconds` on the tombstone ZDO. Component Update's per-frame `_autoRespawnTimer` (which restarted at 0 on every reload) replaced by `MHR_IsAutoRespawnDue()` -> `MHR_RespawnTracker.IsDueForLocalOwner(zdo)`: local player is the owner (RespawnOwnerHash == PlayerID string), alive, and now >= HC_RespawnAt (pre-1.4.46 tombstones get a time stamped on first load).
- New static class `Companions.MHR_RespawnTracker` (appended at end of IL file): pending list in `Player.m_customData["HC_PendingRespawns"]` as ";userID,id,respawnAt" entries; `OnCompanionDied(tombZdo, ownerId)` (called at the end of CreateCompanionTombstone) adds an entry only if the local player is the owner; `Tick()` (from new `CompanionsPlugin.Update`, try/catch, once per second via unscaledTime) respawns due entries whose tombstone ZDO is known but not loaded; loaded ones are left to the component; missing-ZDO entries dropped 3h (world time) past due.
- Config: `AutoRespawnNearOwner` retired; new `Respawn/AutoRespawnAtWorldSpawnWhenNoBed` (default false). Location order: claimed bed -> near owner -> (toggle) world spawn.
- **Design item NOT built - the cross-player hand-off message.** Found while building: a routed "your companion died" message can't help, because when a companion dies on another player's game while its owner is far away, the owner's game never received that tombstone's data, so it has nothing to respawn from. That case falls back to: the owner's game respawns it when the owner comes near the tombstone (respawn time already passed -> immediate). Doing better would need the full respawn data sent in the message and the core reading from a package instead of a ZDO - offered to user as a possible later step.
- check_maxstack.py fixed: methods without an "// end of method" comment (e.g. hand-written ones) are now split at the closing "    }" / next .method. The fixed checker immediately caught 3 new tracker methods at .maxstack 4 needing 5 (all tracker methods set to 8).

Previous version: **1.4.45** — build tag `v63-maxstack-fixes`. Raised .maxstack: MHR_RadialDiag 5->8, MHR_SetBedCollisionIgnored 3->6, UpdateCrafting 8->10, UpdateTakingSeeds 8->10. All checks clean (branches 0/1613, maxstack 0/1613, JIT 1561/1561, BepInPlugin 1.4.45). User's 1.4.44 test (log 26e61b7f): bed label correct, companion in the bed; wheel showed no highlight (MHR_RadialDiag crash every frame in UpdateSelection) and companion wouldn't follow (StopSleeping crashed on its first line -> `_isSleeping` never cleared -> AIGate "blocked by Resting"). Note: the bed-collision helper never actually ran in 1.4.44 (JIT failure) yet the companion was in the bed - so the "floor next to bed" symptom was really the auto-Home overwrite (companion patrolled to its new Home 1.5 m beside the bed). Collision ignore kept anyway (matches vanilla).

Previous version: **1.4.44** — bed/sleep fixes + stance diagnostics. Build tag `v62-bed-sleep-fixes-stance-diag`. Verified: ilasm clean, check_branches 0/1613, JIT 1561/1561, BepInPlugin 1.4.44, `Physics.IgnoreCollision(Collider, Collider, bool)` confirmed in PhysicsModule. Untested in-game.
- **Bed "(Clone)" label regression (user log 3f014ee7):** `CompanionRest.FinalizeSleep` also wrote `_bedClaimNames[bed] = _setup.name` (raw Unity name "HC_Companion(Clone)") every time the companion lay down; 1.4.35 only fixed the claim path (`TryClaimBedIfNeeded` / relink, which use `CompanionInteract.GetCompanionName()`). Whichever write ran last won, so it depended on order. Per user: name is written ONLY at claim. Removed the sleep-time write (the `_claimedBeds.Add` there is kept).
- **Sleeping on the floor beside the bed:** mod already positions at `Bed.m_spawnPoint` every frame, same as vanilla `Player.UpdateAttach`. (My first guess - a missing 0.5 m lift - was WRONG: the (0,0.5,0) in `Bed.Interact` is `detachOffset`, used when getting up.) Real difference: vanilla `Player.AttachStart` calls `Physics.IgnoreCollision(m_collider, eachBedCollider, true)` (undone in AttachStop); the mod didn't, so physics pushed the companion's capsule out of the bed collider. New `CompanionRest.MHR_SetBedCollisionIgnored(Bed, bool)` (companion's CapsuleCollider vs `bed.GetComponentsInChildren<Collider>()`), called with true at the end of FinalizeSleep and false at the start of StopSleeping. Debug log "[Rest] Bed collisions ignored while sleeping / restored (N bed collider(s))".
- **Automatic Home overwrite removed:** FinalizeSleep called `_setup.SetHomePosition(bed + right*1.5, floor-snapped)` on every sleep (log "Bed spawn point set ... (bedside offset)") - legacy original-mod behavior that silently replaced the user's Set Home. Removed; Home now only changes via Set Home. Bed respawn position is still saved separately in FinalizeSleep (`HomeBedPosHash`/`HasHomeBedHash`), unchanged.
- **Stance diagnostics (user: stance selection still feels off - "tilt partway and hold"):** per stick push, one Info line when the stick returns to center: "[Radial] Stick push ended: peak=, time in stance zone=, last stance wedge=, reached outer=, latched stance=". Plus "[Radial] Stance latched: <label> (wedge N)" only when the latch changes. Helpers `MHR_RadialDiag(mag, zone, wedge)` / `MHR_LatchStance(wedge)`. Next step: read the user's pushes to decide between zone-size/hold-time tuning or the D-pad option (JoyDPadUp/Right/Down/Left exist in ZInput; beware D-pad left/right are also hotbar).
- Also this round: stripped trailing whitespace from every line of Companions_source.il (harmless for IL) because mixed trailing spaces were breaking exact-match edits.

Previous version: **1.4.43** — tombstone/respawn redesign **Stage A** (death item rules). Build tag `v61-death-item-rules-stageA`. Verified: ilasm clean, check_branches 0/1610, JIT sweep 1558/1558, BepInPlugin 1.4.43, `ZoneSystem.GetGlobalKey(GlobalKeys)` signature confirmed against game dump. Untested in-game.
- New config `Respawn/DeathItemRule` (int 0-3, default 0): 0 follow world death-penalty keys, 1 keep all, 2 keep equipped, 3 drop all. Int (not named list) because the mod's in-game ConfigPanel only renders float/int/bool/KeyCode rows (user agreed). Old `DropInventoryOnDeath`/`DropEquippedGearOnDeath` fields+binds removed (their lines stay orphaned in existing cfg files, harmless).
- Rule 0 mirrors vanilla `Player.CreateTombStone` exactly (read from the game dump): DeathKeepInventory(0x17) keep all; equipped: DeathKeepEquip(0x13) keep, else DeathDeleteItems(0x14) && !DeathDeleteUnequipped(0x15) delete, else drop; unequipped: DeleteItems||DeleteUnequipped delete, else drop. Helper `CompanionSetup.MHR_DeathItemAction(rule, equipped)` returns 0 keep/1 drop/2 delete.
- Owner's rule travels with the companion: `MHR_StampOwnerDeathRule()` (called from CompanionSetup.Update, throttled to every 2s) writes ZDO int `HC_DeathItemRule` when this game is the ZDO owner AND the local player is the companion's owner (owner string = `Player.GetPlayerID().ToString()`). `MHR_GetDeathItemRule()` uses the stamped value if present, else local config.
- Death split in `CreateCompanionTombstone` rewritten. Found and fixed TWO pre-existing bugs there: (1) keep-inventory was 4x4 (16 slots) vs companion 8x4 (32) - items past 16 stacks were removed from the companion and never added anywhere (deleted); now 8x4, and if AddItem ever fails the item goes back to the live inventory so it drops instead of vanishing; (2) the loop walked `GetAllItems()` (the LIVE list) by index while removing from it, skipping every other item - so in keep-all mode roughly half the "kept" items went to the tombstone. Now iterates a copy. Log line: "[Setup] Death items (rule=N): kept X, deleted Y, the rest go to the tombstone".
- `DoResurrect(isAutoRespawn=true)` no longer pulls items from the tombstone container: sets V_23 (items remain) from the container count, wires the companion's `TombstoneIdHash` so it recovers them itself, logs "[Resurrect] Auto-respawn: left N item(s) in the tombstone...", and jumps to IL_0501. The existing items-remain cleanup already clears RespawnOwner/Prefab on the tombstone ZDO, so the re-attach patch can't resurrect twice. Manual resurrect path unchanged.
- **Stage B still to do** (design agreed with user, see "DESIGNED, NOT YET BUILT: auto-respawn timer" below plus these decisions): persistent world-time deadline on tombstone + owner character data; owner-only tracker on the plugin checking ~1/s; near = existing DoResurrect, far = respawn from saved record with dropped items left in tombstone and tombstone flagged "already respawned"; empty tombstone self-removes next time its area loads (option A, all modes); dead/mid-respawn owner -> wait until alive; offline time on dedicated server counts (respawn immediately on login if due); if another player's game handled the death, it sends the owner's game a routed message, and if owner offline the tombstone respawns on the owner's game when loaded if due. Respawn location: claimed bed -> near owner (default) -> new toggle `AutoRespawnAtWorldSpawnWhenNoBed` (default off) replaces `AutoRespawnNearOwner`; fallbacks only when companion has no bed. Unused original-mod `CompanionManager.QueueRespawn/ProcessRespawns/DoRespawn` queue - leave alone.
- **Separate follow-up raised by user:** the in-game ConfigPanel - "nothing in it can be adjusted." Needs its own investigation later.
- CHANGELOG 1.4.43 entry written (all three packages). Packages NOT rebuilt; README highlights NOT updated (show user first).

Previous version: **1.4.42** — gamepad "sticky stance" selection in the radial menu (resolves the "DIAGNOSED, NOT YET DESIGNED: gamepad stance selection" item below). Design agreed with user: partial stick push toward a stance wedge (up=Balanced, right=Passive, down=Ranged, left=Melee; each a 90° wedge via the existing `ComputeSegmentIndex`) latches that stance after a 0.1s hold (`Time.unscaledDeltaTime`); releasing the stick keeps it highlighted so A/Use applies it (existing `ExecuteSelectedAction` inner path, unchanged). Stance band widened from 0.30–0.55 to 0.30–0.65 stick magnitude. Full push (>=0.65) = outer ring exactly as before, and clears any latched stance. Latch resets every time the menu opens. User explicitly chose the hold-time guard only, NOT a "gesture reached the edge -> no latch until back to center" lockout, because players may push to the edge and then come back to adjust stance in the same motion; the 0.1s hold alone stops the stick's springback from latching a stance by accident. Mouse/keyboard path untouched. Implementation: new fields `_stickyInnerIndex`/`_stanceDwellCandidate`/`_stanceDwellTimer`, new helper `MHR_ApplyStickyInner()`, reset in radial `Show()`, gamepad branch of `UpdateSelection()` rewritten; top-of-method `brfalse.s IL_0083` converted to long form since the gamepad block grew. Build tag `v60-radial-sticky-stance`. Verified: ilasm clean, check_branches 0/1607, JIT sweep 1555/1555, BepInPlugin attribute confirmed 1.4.42. Untested in-game as of delivery. CHANGELOG entries for 1.4.41 and 1.4.42 written (all three packages); packages NOT rebuilt yet, and README Recent Highlights NOT updated yet - show the user the proposed highlights list before packaging (standing rule).

Previous version: **1.4.41** — fixed a Russian-locale startup freeze reported on Hexium (thread 235): `InjectTranslations` (our postfix on Valheim's `Localization.SetupLanguage`) did a full disk-read/JSON-parse/dictionary-rebuild/log every time it fired, with no guard beyond "has the mod initialized at all." The base game calls `SetupLanguage` many times in a row for the same language during startup on some locales (harmless in vanilla, since that's normally cheap) - our postfix turned each of those redundant calls into real work, and on the reporter's system that added up to a multi-minute hang (~1,658 of our injections logged, each pulling in several underlying Unity file-load log lines - ~10,785 total). Fixed by caching the last language actually injected and skipping straight out if `SetupLanguage` fires again for the same language - see "## 1.4.41" below.


## IMPORTANT: version-bump checklist — THREE separate places, not one
Hexium flagged the DLL as still reporting **1.4.37** while every log line and `manifest.json` said 1.4.40 - found the real cause: the version string BepInEx (and Hexium's/Thunderstore's own compatibility checkers) actually reads comes from the `[BepInPlugin(guid, name, version)]` custom attribute on `CompanionsPlugin` (near the top of `Companions_source.il`, a raw byte blob, not a normal string literal - easy to forget). That attribute had not been touched in several versions even though the log strings and changelog kept moving forward, so multiple past releases likely misreported their version to BepInEx/Hexium/Thunderstore without anyone noticing. **Every version bump must update all of the following, or the DLL will lie about its own version:**
1. The `BepInPlugin` custom attribute byte blob (since 1.4.54 the GUID is Maddhattr.OfflineCompanionsReborn - see the 1.4.54 notes for the byte layout) (`.custom instance void class [BepInEx]BepInEx.BepInPlugin::'.ctor'` near the top of the file) - the actual bytes spelling out the version string. Same length in same-length version bumps (e.g. "1.4.40" is 6 bytes like "1.4.37" was) means only the ASCII bytes change, not the length-prefix byte before it - but always double check if the digit count changes (e.g. going from "1.4.9" to "1.4.10").
2. The `PluginVersion` static literal field right below it (currently unused by BepInEx itself, but misleading garbage in the source if left stale - found sitting at "1.3.0" this round, wildly out of date).
3. The `[BUILD]`/`"Offline Companions vX.Y.Z loading..."` log lines (near the top of `CompanionsPlugin::Awake`/cctor, the build-tag strings already tracked per-version in this doc).
4. `manifest.json`'s `version_number` in both `thunderstore_package/` and `hexium_package/`.
5. `CHANGELOG.md` in both packages.
**After any version bump, verify the real embedded version independently of the log strings** - `monodis --customattr Companions.dll | grep -i BepInPlugin` (or `strings -e l Companions.dll | grep -E "^1\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$"`) - don't just trust that bumping the log line means the attribute moved too, since it doesn't.


## CHANGELOG.md style rule (rewritten starting v1.4.40, both packages)
**Current rule (user, after 1.4.74): one line per item, issue and fix combined, to the point.** Example: instead of an "Issue:" bullet ("If a companion couldn't reach a tree or rock, it kept damaging it every 5 seconds from wherever it was, through walls and cliffs.") plus a "Fix:" bullet ("It now gives up on that target and picks another."), write: "Fixed NPC damaging a target regardless of if they could reach it. NPC now picks a new target instead." Rewrite 1.4.53-1.4.74 this way. Older text below is the earlier rule.
User asked for `CHANGELOG.md` to be less technical: plain bullet points describing the issue and the fix in player-facing language, not method/class names, exception types, or root-cause internals (that detail belongs in these dev notes, not the public changelog). Rewrote the entire file in this style, not just new entries going forward - keep new entries in this same plain, bulleted issue/fix format.

## README maintenance rule (applied starting v1.4.40, both packages)
- Added a standing "Note" (right after the ProfMags credit blockquote, near the top) explaining that some work modes (smelting, farming, etc.) are still being rebuilt and may not work correctly, that combat/gear are solid, that regular updates are ongoing, and asking players to report bugs — plus a mention that a way to let players submit their BepInEx `LogOutput.log` is being worked out. This is a standing note, not tied to a specific version - update its wording only if the user asks, don't touch it as part of a routine version bump.
- Added a "## Recent Highlights (vX.Y.Z)" section right under that note: a **very short** (2-4 bullet) list of the current build's most notable changes, in plain player-facing language (not the technical root-cause detail that belongs in `CHANGELOG.md`). **This section must be REPLACED wholesale on every version bump, not appended to** - it always reflects only the current/most recent build, never a growing history. Full detail stays in `CHANGELOG.md` as before. Kept the "See `CHANGELOG.md` for the full version history" line plain - no parenthetical "(short and rotating...)" aside; user flagged that as sounding AI-written.
- **User wants to review this bullet list before it's packaged/posted, every time** - show it in chat and wait for a thumbs up (or edits) before zipping/delivering release packages. Standing instruction, not a one-off.

See "## 1.4.40" below. Per the user: "We may not need to do much more in the way of updates on this [smelting]." The remaining full smelter/kiln rebuild scope (see "IN PROGRESS" section above - ore-first priority, expanding chest search, last-used-chest cache, kiln orchestration speech lines, overburdened/leftover cleanup) is likely superseded by this - the core loop already works end-to-end now that the self-abort bug is gone. **Do NOT restart that rebuild without checking with the user first** - but user explicitly said to keep the plan in these notes, since some of it may still be worth doing, and the same kind of fix (whatever ends up being needed) should probably be applied to the other work modes too, not just Smelt.

## DIAGNOSED, NOT YET DESIGNED: gamepad stance selection (inner ring) is hard to hit

User report (log cfce792e, a clean gamepad session - zero exceptions of any kind, including no repeat of the old `ZInput.GetJoyLeftStickX()` `MissingMethodException` from the 1.4.33 notes, which does not reproduce here): "Selecting a stance is really hard to do. It does not pick up those locations very well... it would only sort of highlight the one for a moment. The outer ring is easy since its a full push of the stick in a circle."

Root cause confirmed via code read of `CompanionRadialMenu.UpdateSelection()`: for gamepad input, which ring is being selected is decided purely by how far the left stick is pushed (magnitude, not held state) -
- magnitude < 0.30 (`StickDeadZone`) -> nothing selected
- **0.30 to 0.55 -> inner ring (combat stances)**
- magnitude >= 0.55 -> outer ring (main actions - Smelt, Follow, etc.)

So outer-ring commands only need "push the stick most/all of the way" (easy, matches the user's experience), but a stance needs the stick held at a deliberately partial push inside a narrow 0.25-wide magnitude band - genuinely hard to land and hold with an analog stick, which explains the "only sort of highlights it for a moment" symptom (the read likely flickers in/out of that band on small thumb tremor). Mouse/KB doesn't have this problem - dead zone is 0-26px, inner ring is a generous 26-162px band there.

**Not yet designed or built - user wants to think about the approach.** Ideas raised so far (not confirmed):
- User's idea: make the highlight "stick" once it lands on a stance (debounce/latch), rather than needing continuous precise stick-magnitude to keep it highlighted.
- User's idea: use the D-pad for stance selection instead of stick-magnitude banding entirely.
- My earlier suggestions (offered, not chosen): widen the inner-ring magnitude band and/or add hysteresis at the boundary; or gate inner-ring access behind a modifier button/trigger instead of analog magnitude.
Discuss and confirm an approach with the user before building anything here.

Previous version: **1.4.39** — real crash found and fixed: `RPC_AddOre` was missing a second argument the game's own `Smelter.RPC_AddOre` requires, causing an `EndOfStreamException` every time a companion inserted ore. Found from the user's own log (not a guess) and confirmed against another actively-maintained Valheim smelter-feeding mod's source calling the same native RPC correctly. See "## 1.4.39" below. Also resolved this round: the `CreateCompanionTombstone` "gear didn't drop" report turned out to be working as designed (see "RESOLVED" below) - no crash reproduced, no fix needed there.

## DESIGNED, NOT YET BUILT: auto-respawn timer breaks when the player wanders away from the tombstone

Confirmed real bug (user's words: "the timer to respawn does not work if you wander far away from the tombstone"). Root cause traced via code read: `CompanionTombstoneResurrect`'s auto-respawn countdown lives in that component's own `Update()` (accumulating `Time.deltaTime` into `_autoRespawnTimer`), and the tombstone `GameObject` is a normal `ZNetView`-scoped object subject to Valheim's distance-based zone unload/culling - so once the player is far enough away, the zone unloads, `Update()` stops being called, and the timer just freezes instead of expiring.

Direction confirmed with user ("That sounds like the right direction"): stop tracking the countdown on the tombstone itself. Persist an absolute deadline in the tombstone's ZDO using `ZNet.GetTimeSeconds()` (persistent world-time; survives both zone unload and a full save/reload, unlike `Time.time`), and maintain a small in-memory registry of pending dead-companion deadlines on `CompanionsPlugin` itself (a MonoBehaviour that isn't ZDO/ZNetView-scoped, so it's never culled) - check it roughly once a second, resurrect directly off ZDO/position data once the deadline passes, no dependency on the tombstone GameObject being loaded nearby.

Confirmed compatible with existing drop/cleanup behavior via code read of `DoResurrect(bool isAutoRespawn)` (the single method both the manual and auto paths already call): it already destroys the tombstone entirely if nothing is left to loot, or leaves it as a plain lootable container (just removing the resurrect component) if items remain - for both resurrection paths. The timer redesign only changes *how the countdown is tracked*, not `DoResurrect` itself, so no leftover-tombstone or gear-drop behavior needs to change. Not yet built - next in line after the smelter rebuild, unless reprioritized.

## IN PROGRESS: Smelter/Kiln full rebuild (design confirmed, build started)

Full design confirmed with user across several rounds (see chat history for the play-by-play). Final agreed design:
- Priority per smelter: **ore first** (own inv -> last-used-chest cache -> expanding chest search up to Set Home radius) -> **then coal** (same source order) -> if no coal found anywhere, **make coal**: find nearby kiln (same expanding search), fuel it with **Wood only** (never FineWood/CoreWood) from own inv/chests, wait for coal output, collect it, return and top off. Keep the smelter fed going forward (don't wait for empty).
- No ore-type priority for now (explicitly deferred as a "nice to have," user's words) - current behavior (first match in the smelter's own `m_conversion` list, i.e. whatever order the game defines) stays as-is.
- Never siphon fuel from a sibling smelter's own supply - confirmed via code read that the existing "_nearbySmelters" loop in `TryPlanFuelRefill` never actually did this anyway (it only summed capacity deficits across same-fuel-type smelters to size one chest trip for multiple smelters at once); that batch-sizing behavior is being dropped per user's request regardless.
- Kiln identification: **CORRECTED from an earlier wrong guess in this same doc.** Originally guessed `m_maxOre == 0 && m_maxFuel > 0` = kiln, and dismissed the debug log's own "kiln"/"furnace" tag (which uses `m_maxFuel == 0`) as backwards/unreliable. A user log (68f046a1) proved that guess wrong and the debug tag *right*: the actual charcoal kiln piece logs as `type=kiln fuel=0/0 ore=0/25` - i.e. **`m_maxFuel == 0` and `m_maxOre > 0`** for a real kiln (no separate fuel slot at all; it burns whatever's in its "ore" slot - Wood - directly into product). A real smelter/furnace logs `type=furnace fuel=17/20 ore=0/10` - both `m_maxFuel > 0` and `m_maxOre > 0`. **Correct signal going forward: `m_maxFuel == 0` = kiln.** This also means the existing `TryPlanRefill` (ore-refill) logic already naturally feeds Wood into a kiln's "ore" slot today, sourced from a chest - confirmed directly in the same log (`Plan: take 25x ore "Wood" ... → "$piece_charcoalkiln"`) - so much more of the "bring wood to make coal" behavior already exists than assumed; the rebuild mainly needs to add the missing pieces (expanding search, cache, fallback-when-truly-nothing-found speech lines, ore-then-coal priority ordering) rather than build kiln-feeding from scratch. Lesson: always re-derive a "confirmed" signal against fresh log evidence before building on it, per the user's standing rule - a plausible guess isn't confirmation.
- Last-used chest cache: per-item-prefab remembered chest, checked before falling back to the existing/rebuilt expanding auto-scan (not a replacement for it).
- No kiln found anywhere (at max search radius): speech lines like "I need a kiln to make coal" / "Where is the kiln" / "We are out of fuel" (rotate), idle-retry - do NOT build a kiln automatically.
- No wood found anywhere for the kiln: speech lines like "I need wood for coal" / "Out of wood" (rotate), idle-retry.
- Speech while running the coal errand (still shown under Smelt work status the whole time, not a separate visible mode): "Making coal" / "I need to refill the smelter" / "Making fuel for the smelter" (rotate).
- Cleanup: leftover coal after filling goes back into the chest it came from (or nearest chest if none); if companion becomes overburdened mid-errand, proactively drop excess coal/ore into a chest.
- Rebuild scope: rebuilding `UpdateScanning`'s priority ordering, `TryPlanFuelRefill` (dropping cross-smelter batch calc, adding kiln sub-task + cache + expanding radius), new kiln-fueling logic, deposit/overburdened logic. Keeping as-is: fuel-item-name derivation from `m_fuelItem`, chest-inventory prefab-match `Find`, weight-capacity math, `RPC_AddFuel`/`RPC_AddOre` calls themselves.
- Building/testing in stages per user's standing preference (ore refill -> coal -> kiln sub-task -> cleanup), not as one giant untested block.

- **Legitimate second abort cause, confirmed not a bug.** The same log that exposed the 1.4.40 self-abort bug also showed a companion's smelt task aborting with "interrupted" (`SmeltController.ShouldAbort()` - combat, or the companion's own interact panel/radial menu open). Traced the exact log moment: `[Interact] Prefix — hold=true arrived, opening radial` / `[Radial] Show ...` right before the abort - the player had opened the companion's radial menu on it mid-insert. Correct, intentional behavior, not related to 1.4.40's bug.
- **User's next design idea, explicitly deferred (not yet discussed in detail or confirmed):** "turning on the smelter should turn off follow. Same for most of the skills. We will have to set that up so that the NPC does not get stuck somehow." I.e. work-mode skills (Smelt, Farm, Harvest, etc.) and Follow should probably be mutually exclusive like Follow/StayHome already are (1.4.38), plus some safety net so a companion can't end up stuck in a work mode with no way out. Needs its own design discussion before building - do not build from this note alone.
- **Same self-abort *pattern* may exist elsewhere, not yet investigated.** `NotifyControllersInventoryChanged()` also unconditionally calls `FarmController.NotifyActionModeChanged()` (harmless - just resets a scan timer/flags, doesn't abort anything) and `HarvestController.NotifyActionModeChanged()` (calls `CancelDirectedTarget()` and either `ExitGatherMode()` or `ResetToIdle()` - more disruptive than Farm's, though Harvest normally *adds* items to inventory rather than consuming its own, so it may rarely self-trigger in practice). Left untouched for this fix since it wasn't the reported bug and hasn't been confirmed as an actual in-game problem - worth keeping in mind if a similar "harvest task keeps getting interrupted for no reason" report ever comes in.

## RESOLVED

0. **"Gear didn't drop on death" report (v1.4.39, no code change needed).** User's log (68f046a1) shows the mod working exactly as configured both times a companion died: `DropInventoryOnDeath`/`DropEquippedGearOnDeath` both true in their config, and the log confirms `Creating tombstone for "Companion" — 3 items` / `Grave transfer complete — 3 items in tombstone` (and 4 items the second death). In both cases, within roughly 10-30 real seconds, `[Resurrect] Transferred N items from tombstone to companion` fires and the companion is back up with everything - at the *same position* it died, with no `[Interact] hold threshold reached` log line nearby (that tag is used for the radial-menu hold, not necessarily a tombstone-resurrect hold, so it doesn't rule out the user manually resurrecting). Told the user: the drop worked, and it looks like it got recovered almost immediately either by them holding resurrect at the tombstone, or the companion auto-recovering its own very-nearby tombstone - asked them to confirm which so we know whether anything (e.g. the auto-respawn timer firing far earlier than its configured 300s) actually needs fixing.

1. **`RPC_AddOre` missing its second argument, crashing ore insertion (v1.4.39).** See "## 1.4.39" section above for full root-cause and fix detail.

2. **Follow vs. Stay Home priority (v1.4.38).** Root cause confirmed via code read: `ApplyFollowMode`'s mode=0 (Follow) case checked `stayHome` first and unconditionally patrolled home if true, ignoring the `follow` flag; separately `CompanionRadialMenu.DoSetHome` auto-enabled StayHome as a side effect. Fixed: `ToggleFollow()` now also clears StayHome when turning Follow on; `ToggleStayHome()` now also clears Follow when turning StayHome on; `DoSetHome()` no longer forces StayHome on (only sets home position, and re-patrols immediately only if StayHome was already on). Verified: `ilasm` clean compile, `check_branches.py` 0/1606 problems, `monodis --typedef` OK, JIT-prep sweep `TOTAL=1554 FAILED=0`.

Previous version: **1.4.34** — fixed the real cause of three symptoms reported together after the 1.4.32/1.4.33 bed-claim rollout (companions not sleeping, "Set Home" seeming to fail, erratic movement near beds): `Bed.GetOwner()`/`GetOwnerName()`/`IsMine()` are all `private` in the game's own assembly, so the direct IL calls to `Bed.GetOwner()` from `FindNearbyBed`, `FindUnclaimedBedNear`, and `HasOwnerBedNearby` had never actually worked - they threw `MethodAccessException` every time, confirmed repeated dozens of times in the user's log. `ilasm` doesn't check member accessibility (a third instance of this pattern, after `.maxstack` and short-branch-range). Fixed via a reflective wrapper (`CompanionRest.GetBedOwnerSafe`, using `HarmonyLib.AccessTools.Method` + `MethodInfo.Invoke`, same house style as the existing private-field access in `ModLocalization`), routing all 6 call sites through it. See "## 1.4.34" section below for full root-cause tracing and fix details. Untested in-game as of delivery.

Previous version: **1.4.33** — fixed a real, silent-until-runtime crash class: hand-editing the IL over many versions had, in several places, shifted a short-form branch (`brtrue.s`/`brfalse.s`/`bne.un.s`, 1-byte signed displacement, range -128..127) far enough from its target that the displacement went out of range. Neither `ilasm` (which does not validate this) nor the JIT-preparation sweep from 1.4.31/1.4.32 (which also doesn't reliably catch it, confirmed by deliberately reintroducing a known-bad case and rerunning the sweep — 0 failures reported) catches this; it only surfaces at runtime, as `VerificationException: ... Invalid instruction target <garbage hex>`, and only for players whose exact build happened to load/JIT the affected method (explaining why some players hit it and others on the same version didn't). Found by writing a from-scratch CIL byte-offset calculator (`check_branches.py`) that walks every method, computes real instruction sizes, and flags any short branch whose target displacement exceeds the signed-byte range — then running it against the *entire* codebase rather than just the one method a user's log named. It found 10 out-of-range short branches across 7 methods, including the reported `CompanionRest.FindNearbyBed`, plus `CreateCompanionTombstone`, `UpdateTombstoneRecovery`, `DirectSleep`, `FinalizeSleep`, `TryClaimBedIfNeeded`, and `HomesteadController.DoRepairBuildings` (this last one hadn't been reported yet). All ten fixed by converting the short-form branch to its long form (4-byte displacement) at each flagged site; `ilasm` recomputes the actual offsets correctly once the mnemonic no longer forces the 1-byte encoding. Re-ran `check_branches.py` after the fix: 0 problems found across all 1605 methods. Compiled clean via `ilasm`, `--typedef`/`strings` checks pass, and the JIT-preparation sweep is still 1553/1553 clean (expected — it was never going to catch this bug class; it's just the existing baseline check, not proof this fix worked). **`check_branches.py` should be kept and rerun after any future hand-edit near a short branch** — this is now the second class of `ilasm`-invisible bug found (the first was the `.maxstack` issue in 1.4.31), and both were caused by inserting code near existing instructions without re-deriving the affected size-dependent values by hand. Still open from the same log: `MissingMethodException` on `ZInput.GetJoyLeftStickX()` in `CompanionRadialMenu.Update()` (likely the reported "wonky" Xbox controller cursor), and an `EndOfStreamException` in `ZPackage.ReadBool()` during `SmeltController.UpdateInsertingItem` — neither investigated yet.

Previous version: **1.4.32** — bed claims are now persistent, player-like standing assignments instead of a transient "occupied right now" state (see "## 1.4.32" section below for full design/implementation notes). A companion's claimed bed always shows "(claimed by \<name\>)" and is their auto-respawn point; they claim one automatically on Set Home, via a ~30s background check, or by noticing their owner has a bed nearby; pointing-and-commanding a bed is now the manual override (claim/sleep/refuse-with-a-line); claiming a new bed releases the old one; and they no longer wander to a random nearby bed at night if they have no claim. Compiled clean via `ilasm`, verified via a full JIT-preparation sweep of every method in the assembly (1553/1553 clean) in addition to the usual `--typedef`/`strings` checks, untested in-game as of delivery.

## 1.4.41 — localization re-injection guard (Russian-locale startup freeze)

**Background.** Hexium bug report (thread 235): game hangs on the loading screen for ~2 minutes then exits with no crash dialog or exceptions, on Windows 64-bit Steam with the system language set to Russian, BepInExPack Valheim 5.4.2350, mod v1.4.40. Reporter attached log excerpts (not the full log) showing ~1,658 of our own `[Localization] Injected N defaults + N "Russian" overrides` lines and ~10,785 Unity `Loaded localization file #N` lines, all within the ~2-minute window before exit. Reporter noted the original (pre-fork) Offline Companions mod reportedly had a similar startup freeze fixed in its 1.0.2-1.0.3, suggesting this is a known category of bug, not a one-off.

**Root cause.** Disassembled Valheim's own `Localization` class (`assembly_guiutils.dll`) to see who actually calls `SetupLanguage` and how often. Confirmed: our mod never calls `SetupLanguage`/`SetLanguage` directly (checked - only ever reads `GetSelectedLanguage()`), so we are not the one driving the repeat calls; that comes from vanilla game code (constructor, `SetStartupLanguage`, locale detection, `SetLanguage`'s public API) and, on certain locales, some engine/platform interaction evidently calls it far more than the couple of times you'd expect at a normal boot. In vanilla Valheim, a repeated `SetupLanguage` call is fairly cheap (it iterates a small, fixed list of localization CSV assets already loaded into memory). But our `SetupLanguage_Postfix` (a Harmony postfix that runs after every single call) had no guard beyond "has the mod's `Init()` run at all" - so every one of those repeated calls also did a full disk read of both `English.json` and `Russian.json` from the plugin's Translations folder, JSON parsing, a full dictionary rebuild via reflection, and a `LogInfo` call. At ~1,658 repeats, that's what turned a vanilla quirk into an observed multi-minute freeze - we can't fix (and don't need to explain) why the base game calls `SetupLanguage` so many times on this locale; we only need our own response to a repeat to be cheap instead of expensive.

**Fix.** Added a new static `_lastInjectedLanguage` field to `ModLocalization`. `InjectTranslations` now compares the currently-selected language against it right after fetching `GetSelectedLanguage()`, before any of the expensive work - if unchanged, it returns immediately (one string comparison, no file I/O, no logging); if changed, it proceeds exactly as before and updates the cached value. This is the fix the reporter themselves suggested (guard re-injection so it only does real work when the language actually changes) and matches the pattern the original mod apparently already needed for the same class of issue.

Build tag `v59-localization-reinject-guard-fix`, version 1.4.41 (`1.4.40`→`1.4.41`). Verified: `ilasm` clean compile, `check_branches.py` 0/1606 problems, `monodis --typedef` OK, JIT-prep sweep `TOTAL=1554 FAILED=0`, and this time also independently confirmed the real embedded version via `monodis --customattr` / `strings` (not just the log line) per the version-bump checklist above. Untested in-game as of delivery, and in particular **not tested on an actual Russian-locale setup** (the reporting player's exact scenario) - next test should ideally come from that same player re-testing, since this specific interaction couldn't be reproduced locally without their locale/platform setup.

## 1.4.40 — smelt task self-aborting on its own inventory changes ("mode changed")

**Background.** User's log (f2d4c947) showed real progress on the still-in-progress smelter rebuild: a companion found the kiln and started feeding it Wood - but the log showed the kiln getting stuck at `ore=1/25` for many repeated cycles, never advancing far enough to reach the actual smelter's ore-loading step. Reported by the user as "The NPC did start the kiln. They did not load ore into the smelter though."

**Root cause.** `CompanionSetup.OnInventoryChanged()` (fires whenever the companion's own inventory changes, for any reason) calls `CompanionSetup.NotifyControllersInventoryChanged()`, which checks the companion's current `ActionModeHash` ZDO value and, if it's `6` (Smelt), unconditionally calls `SmeltController.NotifyActionModeChanged()` - which unconditionally `Abort("mode changed")`s whenever a smelt task is in progress. The problem: **inserting ore/fuel into a kiln or smelter is itself an inventory change** (the item leaves the companion's inventory) - so the very act of doing the smelt task's job re-triggered this check and aborted the insert it was in the middle of. Log evidence matched exactly: kiln got 1 wood in, went `active=True`, then `Aborted — mode changed (phase was InsertingItem)` / `Restored follow target to player`, repeating on every subsequent insert attempt.

Confirmed this was a redundant, buggy second notification path, not the real one: the genuine mode-change notification already happens correctly in `CompanionRadialMenu.SetActionMode(int mode)` - the method that actually runs when the player picks a new job off the radial menu. It writes the new mode to the ZDO and calls `NotifyActionModeChanged()` on every relevant controller (Harvest, Smelt, Farm, Fish, Cook) directly, unconditionally, on every real click - which is the correct time to do it, since a real mode change genuinely did just happen. `NotifyControllersInventoryChanged()`'s Smelt branch served no purpose beyond duplicating that same call on the wrong trigger (any inventory change, not an actual mode change).

**Fix.** Removed the `SmeltController.NotifyActionModeChanged()` call and its surrounding `GetComponent`/mode-check block from `NotifyControllersInventoryChanged()`. The method's `FarmController` and `HarvestController` branches (and the rest of the method) are untouched - see "STILL OPEN / DEFERRED" above for a note on why those weren't touched in this pass.

Also investigated a second abort reason visible in the same log ("interrupted") - confirmed legitimate (user opened the companion's radial menu mid-insert), not related to this bug. See "STILL OPEN / DEFERRED" above.

Build tag `v58-smelt-selfabort-inventorychanged-fix`, version 1.4.40 (`1.4.39`→`1.4.40`). Verified: `ilasm` clean compile, `check_branches.py` 0/1606 problems, `monodis --typedef` OK, JIT-prep sweep `TOTAL=1554 FAILED=0`, `strings -e l` confirms build tag/version. Untested in-game as of delivery - next test should specifically watch a companion feed a kiln/smelter through to completion (multiple inserts in a row) without the follow-target-restored/abort log spam.

## 1.4.39 — RPC_AddOre missing its second argument, crashing ore insertion

**Background.** User reported "Smelting looks like it had some issue" with a fresh log, plus a separate report that gear didn't seem to drop on companion death despite the config toggle being on.

**Crash root cause, confirmed from the log.** Right after `[Smelt|Companion] Arrived at smelter interact point (dist=3.3m) — inserting 6x IronScrap`, the log shows three repeated `EndOfStreamException: Unable to read beyond the end of the stream` errors, stack-traced through `ZPackage.ReadBool()` → `ZRpc.Deserialize` → `ZNetView.Deserialize` → `Companions.SmeltController.UpdateInsertingItem`. Reading the IL: `UpdateInsertingItem`'s ore-insert branch calls `InvokeRPC("RPC_AddOre", object[]{ _carryingItemPrefab })` - a single-element array, just the item name. The `ReadBool` failure means the RPC handler on the other end expects to deserialize a `bool` that was never sent.

**Verification against a real, current reference.** Rather than guess at the missing parameter, I checked another actively-maintained Valheim mod that also feeds smelters via the same native RPCs (`themockingjet/SmelterAutoFeed` on GitHub, cloned and inspected directly). Its `StationFeedService.TryFeedOre` calls `smelterView.InvokeRPC("RPC_AddOre", ore.m_dropPrefab.name, false)` - two arguments: the item name **and a bool** (almost certainly the secondary-conversion flag Valheim added for recipes like the blast furnace's alternate ore types). Its `RPC_AddFuel` call, by contrast, takes zero extra arguments (`InvokeRPC("RPC_AddFuel")`), matching all four of our own `RPC_AddFuel` call sites exactly - so fuel insertion was never broken, only ore.

**Fix.** Changed the `RPC_AddOre` call site in `UpdateInsertingItem` to build a 2-element `object[]` instead of 1: `{ _carryingItemPrefab, false }` (boxed `System.Boolean`), matching the verified-correct reference call.

**Build verification.** `ilasm` reassembled clean; `check_branches.py` 0 problems across 1606 methods; `monodis --typedef` OK; `strings -e l` confirms `v57-smelt-rpcaddore-missing-arg-fix` / `Offline Companions v1.4.39 loading...`; JIT-preparation sweep `TOTAL=1554 FAILED=0`. Delivered to the user as a bare DLL (packaging still deferred - see 1.4.38 notes).

**Not yet fixed:** the broader smelter/kiln ore-first/coal/kiln-orchestration rebuild (IN PROGRESS section above) - this was a separate, narrower crash fix found while investigating the user's new log, done first since it's a real crash with a confirmed, verified root cause.

## 1.4.38 — Follow/StayHome made mutually exclusive; Set Home no longer forces StayHome on

**Fix, confirmed with the user before building.** Two small, isolated edits plus one removal, all in `CompanionRadialMenu`:
- `ToggleFollow()`: after `SetFollow(V_0)`, if the new value is true (Follow turning on), also call `_companion.SetStayHome(false)`.
- `ToggleStayHome()`: after `SetStayHome(V_1)`, if the new value is true (StayHome turning on), also call `_companion.SetFollow(false)`.
- `DoSetHome()`: removed the `if (!GetStayHome()) SetStayHome(true)` force entirely. It still sets home position, and — only if StayHome was already true going in — immediately clears the follow target and re-patrols to the new home position (so an already-staying-home companion snaps to the new spot right away); it no longer flips StayHome as a side effect of setting a home point.

No changes needed in `ApplyFollowMode` itself - once the two toggles are kept properly exclusive at the source, its existing priority check (`stayHome` checked before `follow` in the mode=0 case) becomes correct by construction, since the two flags can no longer both be true at once via the radial menu.

**Build verification.** `ilasm` reassembled clean; `check_branches.py` 0 problems across 1606 methods; `monodis --typedef` OK; `strings -e l` confirms `v56-follow-stayhome-exclusive` / `Offline Companions v1.4.38 loading...`; JIT-preparation sweep `TOTAL=1554 FAILED=0`. Delivered to the user as a bare DLL (not packaged/zipped - user is testing directly and separately trying to repro the `CreateCompanionTombstone` death-crash report while the smelter/kiln rebuild continues in parallel; see "IN PROGRESS" section above).

## 1.4.37 — re-entrant Abort() during chest pickup crashes UpdateTakingFromChest

**Background.** After 1.4.36, the user reported smelting was "sort of better" - the companion did finally pick up iron ore - but still ran around unable to find where to deposit it, and asked for a broader rebuild of the fuel/ore logic (see OPEN DESIGN DISCUSSION above). They also hit a real crash while testing something unrelated (dropping gear on death), and separately found the companion wouldn't Follow after re-enabling it.

**Crash root cause, confirmed from the log.** `[Error : Unity Log] NullReferenceException ... Companions.SmeltController.UpdateTakingFromChest ()`. The log immediately before it: `"Setup] OnInventoryChanged — auto-equip triggered"` (from the companion picking up IronScrap), then `[EQUIPDIAG]`/auto-equip lines, then `"[Smelt|Companion] NotifyActionModeChanged — aborting smelt"`, `"Aborted — mode changed (phase was TakingFromChest)"`, `"Restored patrol to home"`, then `"Took 6x \"\" (total taken=6)"` - and then the crash. That last line is the tell: `_carryingItemPrefab` had already been cleared to empty string by `Abort()` by the time the take-loop's own logging ran, meaning `Abort()` executed **in the middle of** the take-loop's single method call, re-entrantly - triggered synchronously by `Inventory.AddItem()`'s `OnInventoryChanged` side effect, not as a separate frame/Update() call. `Abort()` clears `_targetChest` to null as part of its cleanup; the take-loop's own finalize step (reached right after the loop, once the abort's re-entrant call chain unwound back into it) unconditionally does `_targetChest.SetInUse(false)` - on a now-null reference. Confirmed by reading the actual IL: this specific `callvirt Container::SetInUse(bool)` right after the loop is the only unguarded null-dereference candidate in the method; everything else already null-checks (e.g. `_targetSmelter` is checked via `op_Inequality` before use a few lines later).

**Fix.** Added a null-check on `_targetChest` immediately after the take-loop, before the `SetInUse(false)` call: if it's already null (meaning a re-entrant `Abort()` ran underneath the loop), just return immediately - `Abort()` already did whatever cleanup was needed, so there's nothing left to safely do here. Verified via `check_branches.py` that this didn't break either of the two existing short-form branches (`br.s`/`blt.s`) that target the same label the fix now sits in front of - both still resolve correctly and remain in range after the insertion.

**Build verification.** `ilasm` reassembled clean; `check_branches.py` reports 0 problems across all 1606 methods; `monodis --typedef`/`strings` (`v55-smelt-reentrant-abort-npe-fix`, `Offline Companions v1.4.37 loading...`) pass; JIT-preparation sweep 1554/1554 clean. Untested in-game as of delivery.

**Not yet fixed or built this round:** the smelter fuel/ore/chest-search redesign (deferred to a dedicated design discussion, per the user's own request and standing preference for discussing tradeoffs before building), and the Follow/StayHome interaction bug (diagnosed below but not yet discussed with the user).

**Follow-vs-StayHome diagnosis (not yet fixed).** The user: "The NPC would not follow me any more when I re-enabled that feature." Log showed `stayHome=True` on every single `ApplyFollowMode` call for the entire session, including every time `follow` was toggled true via the radial "Follow" button. Reading `ApplyFollowMode`'s mode=0 branch: it checks `stayHome` (computed as `GetStayHome() && HasHomePosition()`) *first*, and if true, unconditionally sets the companion to patrol at home - the `follow` flag is never even consulted in that branch. Separately, `CompanionRadialMenu.DoSetHome` (the "Set Home" button) auto-enables StayHome as a side effect if it wasn't already on (`if (!GetStayHome()) SetStayHome(true)`). So: the user pressed Set Home at some point (silently turning StayHome on), and later toggling Follow back on had no visible effect, because StayHome - still on, invisibly - takes priority over Follow by design in `ApplyFollowMode`. This is a design question, not a clear bug: worth asking the user whether Follow should override/clear StayHome, whether Set Home should stop auto-enabling it, or both, before touching this code.

## 1.4.36 — smelt stuck-detection didn't catch "wandering without progress"

**Background.** After 1.4.35, the user tested again: sleeping and Set Home both continued working fine (confirmed - no exceptions anywhere in that log), but the bed-claim hover text looked wrong ("Unclaimed (claimed by HC_Companion(Clone))" - see 1.4.35 above) and smelting "does not work at all - the NPC just runs around looking for materials maybe."

**Diagnosis.** No exceptions anywhere related to smelting either - this was a pure behavior bug. The log showed the companion repeatedly picking a chest, then its logged distance to that chest bouncing around for many ticks (11.3m → 8.9m → 12.2m → 10.5m → 9.1m → ... → 4.4m) with real nonzero velocity readings throughout, before plateauing at 4.4m - short of the 2.5m interaction range - for several more ticks, at which point the player opened the radial menu (itself an interrupt) and re-tried, with the same result on each of 3 consecutive attempts. `SmeltController.UpdateStuckDetection` already existed to catch exactly this kind of failure (accumulate a timer once genuinely stuck, then blacklist the position for 30s via the existing `CompanionAI._targetBlacklist` and abort to try a different chest) - but its definition of "stuck" was "moved less than 0.5m in the last real second," checked via `Vector3.Distance(transform.position, _stuckCheckPos)` against a position snapshot taken every ~1s. A companion that's genuinely wandering/oscillating near an obstacle (real velocity, just not net progress toward the goal) satisfies that "moved enough" check on most ticks, so `_stuckTimer` kept resetting to 0 and the 10-second threshold for triggering recovery was never actually reached - the player lost patience and manually interrupted first, and since the blacklist never got applied, retrying just sent the companion right back to the same chest.

**Discussed with the user before implementing** (per their standing preference for design tradeoffs to be confirmed before code changes): offered three options - track progress-toward-target instead of raw movement; just shorten the existing 10s raw-movement timeout; or both. User chose progress-tracking, and separately asked whether the resulting blacklist has its own expiry so a chest isn't permanently avoided - confirmed it already does (`CompanionAI.IsPositionBlacklisted` already expires entries after 30s via a stored `Time.time` timestamp, unchanged, no new work needed there).

**Fix.** Added a new field, `SmeltController._stuckBestDist` (the best/smallest distance-to-target seen since the last `ResetStuck()`), initialized to `float.MaxValue` in `ResetStuck()` alongside the existing stuck-tracking fields (`ResetStuck()` is already called at all 9 phase/target-transition call sites, so no other call sites needed touching). Rewrote the core check in `UpdateStuckDetection`: each ~1-second tick, it now computes the real distance from the companion to `GetCurrentTargetPosition()` (the same helper already used later in the method for blacklisting) and compares it against `_stuckBestDist` - if it's improved by at least 0.3m (a small margin against positional noise), that becomes the new best distance and the stuck-timer resets to 0; otherwise the stuck-timer accumulates exactly as before. The 10-second threshold, the blacklist call, and the abort-and-retry-a-different-chest behavior are all unchanged - only the definition of "not making progress" changed, from raw self-movement to actual progress toward the goal.

**Build verification.** `ilasm` reassembled clean; `check_branches.py` reports 0 problems across all 1606 methods; manually traced the new code's stack depth (peaks at 2) against the method's existing `.maxstack 3` - no bump needed; `monodis --typedef`/`strings` (`v54-smelt-stuck-progress-check`, `Offline Companions v1.4.36 loading...`) pass; the JIT-preparation sweep is 1554/1554 clean. As always, this sweep doesn't validate the actual behavior change (it never has for any of these bug classes) - untested in-game as of delivery.

**Noted, not acted on:** the exact same stuck-detection pattern (raw self-displacement, `_stuckTimer`/`_stuckCheckTimer`/`_stuckCheckPos`, a 10s threshold) is hand-duplicated across several other controllers (Farm, Repair, Homestead, and others - found via grep, not individually inspected). Only `SmeltController` was reported and fixed this round; the same "wanders near a cluttered target without ever triggering recovery" failure could in principle affect those too, but wasn't touched without a concrete report to confirm against.

## 1.4.35 — bed-claim hover text: wrong name, and reads like the bed isn't actually claimed

**Background.** Same test session as 1.4.36 above. The user reported the bed-claim hover text "looks terrible, it still says 'Unclaimed' then something about a 'clone' - not really what I was thinking. It should really look like the bed is claimed even if it is not. It also should say companion or the companion assigned name."

**Root cause, two separate bugs.** (1) `CompanionRest.TryClaimBedIfNeeded` was storing `_setup.get_name()` - Unity's own `UnityEngine.Object.name` property, i.e. the raw GameObject name (e.g. `HC_Companion(Clone)`, since Unity auto-appends `(Clone)` to anything instantiated from a prefab) - into `_bedClaimNames`, instead of using the mod's own existing, correct name lookup. `CompanionInteract.GetCompanionName()` already existed and does this properly (reads the player-assigned name from the ZDO via `CompanionSetup.NameHash`, falling back to the vanilla character name or a localized default) - it just wasn't being used here, and was `private` so `CompanionRest` couldn't call it directly. (2) The Harmony postfix on `Bed.GetHoverText` (`BedClaimHoverTextPatch.Postfix`) built its text as `"{0} (claimed by {1})"`, where `{0}` was the *original* vanilla hover text. Since the real, underlying `Bed` ownership is still 0 (this is a cosmetic-only fake, by design - see the 1.4.30/1.4.32 notes on why), vanilla's own `GetHoverText()` returns `"Unclaimed\n[E] Claim bed"` for an "unclaimed" bed - so the result read as the nonsensical "Unclaimed\n[E] Claim bed (claimed by HC_Companion(Clone))".

**Fix.** Made `CompanionInteract.GetCompanionName()` `assembly`-visible (same fix pattern as the 1.4.34 `Bed.GetOwner()` issue, just applied to the mod's own code this time, where changing accessibility is completely safe) and had `TryClaimBedIfNeeded` call it instead of the raw `.name` property. Rewrote the hover-text postfix to fully replace the vanilla text with `"{0}'s Bed"` (matching the exact format a genuinely player-owned bed shows, per the real `Bed.GetHoverText()` disassembly: `"<OwnerName>'s $piece_bed"`) rather than appending onto it.

**Build verification.** `ilasm` reassembled clean; `check_branches.py` clean; `strings -e l` confirms the new format string (`"{0}'s Bed"`) and build tag (`v53-bed-claim-display-name-fix`) are baked in; JIT-preparation sweep 1554/1554 clean. Untested in-game as of delivery (confirmed working as of the next test, alongside the 1.4.36 work above).

## 1.4.34 — Bed.GetOwner() is private: the real root cause of "sleep/Set Home/pathing all broken at once"

**Background.** After delivering 1.4.33, the user tested it (confirmed by the log's own build-tag string: `v51-branch-range-fix`, `Offline Companions v1.4.33 loading...` - so this was genuinely the branch-range-fixed build, not a stale one) and reported three symptoms together: sleeping doesn't work, companions run around erratically even with an unclaimed bed right in front of them, and Set Home doesn't work anymore. They also attached a fresh `Managed.zip`, since a same-day Valheim patch (still 1.0.16) had landed and they suspected it might be the cause.

**Root cause.** The log was full (dozens of repeats) of: `MethodAccessException: Method 'Bed.GetOwner()' is inaccessible from method 'Companions.CompanionRest.FindUnclaimedBedNear(UnityEngine.Vector3,single)'`. Disassembling the freshly-uploaded `assembly_valheim.dll` confirmed: `Bed.GetOwner()`, `Bed.GetOwnerName()`, and `Bed.IsMine()` are all declared `.method private hidebysig` - genuinely private, not internal or protected. There is no public accessor for a bed's ownership on the `Bed` type at all. The mod's bed-claim code (`FindNearbyBed`, `FindUnclaimedBedNear`, `HasOwnerBedNearby` - all added in 1.4.32) called `Bed.GetOwner()` directly via a normal `callvirt` IL instruction, which requires the caller to have access to the member. Since the mod assembly has no such access, this throws `MethodAccessException` **every single time**, and always would have - this is not something today's game patch changed; it's been broken since 1.4.32 first shipped, just never exercised against the actual game until now. `ilasm` does not check member accessibility across assemblies at compile time, so this passed every previous build check (including the branch-range scan, which only concerns itself with in-method jump targets, not cross-assembly call legality) completely clean. This is now the third distinct class of "compiles fine, breaks at runtime" bug found in three rounds - `.maxstack` (1.4.31), short-branch range (1.4.33), and now member accessibility (1.4.34) - none of which `ilasm`, `monodis`, or the `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` JIT-sweep harness catch.

**Why this explains all three reported symptoms, not three separate bugs.** All three affected methods sit early in call chains that run every relevant frame or every player action:
- `FindNearbyBed` is called from `TryAutoSleep`, which runs whenever a companion should be trying to sleep - so the exception fires (and, being uncaught, cuts short whatever runs after it in that call) essentially continuously while a companion is anywhere near sleep-eligible conditions. This directly explains "sleeping does not work."
