- `HasOwnerBedNearby` and `FindUnclaimedBedNear` are both called from `TryClaimBedIfNeeded`, which runs both on the ~30s periodic timer inside `CompanionRest.Update()` *and* synchronously from `CompanionSetup.SetHomePosition` (confirmed by reading the actual call site - `SetHomePosition` already writes the ZDO home-position fields *before* calling `TryClaimBedIfNeeded`, so the position itself was very likely still being saved; the exception firing immediately after is a much more plausible explanation for "seems to fail" - e.g. a confirmation line or other follow-up the player expects never runs - than the position write itself failing).
- The user's own description - "the NPC is running all over the place when there is an unclaimed bed right in front of them" - fits a companion whose per-frame bed/sleep check keeps throwing partway through, potentially leaving it in a half-decided state repeatedly, rather than a distinct pathing regression.

No other exceptions or errors appear anywhere else in the log (verified by grepping the whole file for `Error`/`Exception`, filtering out the by-then-expected repeats), and the pre-existing `InitSkillDefs` warning is the known, already-handled one. So this one bug is treated as the complete explanation, not a partial one, pending the user's next test.

**Fix.** Added a new static field `CompanionRest._bedGetOwnerMethod` (a cached `System.Reflection.MethodInfo`), resolved once in `CompanionRest`'s static constructor via `HarmonyLib.AccessTools.Method(typeof(Bed), "GetOwner", null, null)` - the same reflective-access pattern the mod already uses elsewhere (`ModLocalization` resolves a couple of private fields on the game's own `Localization` class via `AccessTools.Field`). Added a new private static helper, `CompanionRest.GetBedOwnerSafe(Bed bed)`, that null-checks both the bed and the cached `MethodInfo`, then calls `MethodInfo.Invoke(bed, null)` inside a `try`/`catch(Exception)` that returns `0` (treated as "no owner") on any failure rather than ever letting an exception escape. All 6 of the mod's direct `Bed.GetOwner()` call sites (`FindNearbyBed`×1, `DirectSleep`×1, `FindUnclaimedBedNear`×1, `HasOwnerBedNearby`×1, `TryClaimBedIfNeeded`×2) were replaced with calls to this helper - a straightforward swap, since a static helper taking the `Bed` as its one argument has the identical calling-convention shape (pop one object reference, push one `int64`) as the direct instance `callvirt` it replaces, so no other code at each call site needed to change.

**Build verification.** `ilasm` reassembled clean; `check_branches.py` still reports 0 problems across all 1606 methods (confirms the new helper method's own branches are in range); `monodis --typedef` and `strings -e l` (build tag `v52-bed-getowner-access-fix`, version string `Offline Companions v1.4.34 loading...`) pass; the JIT-preparation sweep, rerun against the *freshly uploaded* `Managed` folder (today's game patch) rather than the previously-cached one, reports `TOTAL=1554 FAILED=0`. As with the two earlier bug classes, a clean sweep here is not proof the fix works at runtime - this harness has never reliably caught any of these three bug classes - so this remains untested in-game as of delivery, and should be confirmed on the user's next session rather than assumed fixed.

**Also checked, found clean:** re-examined the whole log line-by-line for any other new error introduced by today's Valheim 1.0.16 content patch (the user's stated worry) - found none. The `VerificationException` class of bug from 1.4.33 did not reappear, confirming that fix held under the new game build.

## 1.4.33 — branch-range crash fix (found via `check_branches.py`, a new static-analysis tool)

**Background.** After delivering 1.4.32, the user tested an *older* build (v1.4.31, confirmed via `strings -e l` on their uploaded DLL — the build tag baked in was `v49b-auto-respawn-maxstack-fix`, not the newly-delivered 1.4.32's `v50-persistent-bed-claims`) and hit a new crash: `VerificationException: Error in Companions.CompanionRest:FindNearbyBed () Invalid instruction target ffffffc5`. `0xffffffc5` as a signed int32 is `-59` — consistent with a corrupted/out-of-range short-branch displacement, not a random memory error.

**Root cause.** CIL short-form branches (`br.s`, `brtrue.s`, `brfalse.s`, `beq.s`, `bne.un.s`, etc.) encode their target as a **signed single byte** (-128..127), relative to the offset immediately after the branch instruction. Across many rounds of hand-editing this file (inserting new instructions near existing branches without re-checking the byte distance to their targets), several of these had drifted out of range. Critically: **`ilasm` does not validate this at assembly time** — it silently emits whatever byte value the (now out-of-range, wrapped/truncated) displacement computes to, producing a DLL that assembles "successfully" but is invalid. This is the exact same class of silent failure as the 1.4.31 `.maxstack` bug, just a different mechanism.

**Verification tooling gap, confirmed empirically (again).** Reran `verify_all.exe` (the `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod`-based JIT-sweep harness built during the 1.4.31 hotfix) directly against the user's own uploaded, crashing DLL: `TOTAL=1546 FAILED=0`. Zero failures reported, despite the DLL provably crashing in the real game. So neither of the two "verify without playing the game" tools available (`ilasm`, `verify_all.exe`) catches this bug class. This matches the earlier `.maxstack` finding: Mono's normal JIT path for a directly-loaded, trusted assembly doesn't enforce full IL verification the way Harmony's dynamic-method patching (or, presumably, whatever code path `FindNearbyBed` happens to go through in the live game) does.

**New tool: `check_branches.py`.** Since neither existing tool could locate (let alone prevent) this bug class, wrote a from-scratch static analyzer:
- Parses every `.method` block out of `Companions_source.il`.
- Walks each method's instructions in source order, computing the *real* CIL byte size of each one from a hand-built opcode table (1-byte vs. `0xFE`-prefixed 2-byte opcodes; correct operand sizes per mnemonic, including the `switch` instruction's variable-length target list and the fact that `ldstr`'s multi-line bytearray hex-dump in the `.il` source is a display convention only — the actual encoded instruction is always exactly 5 bytes, independent of string length).
- Tracks each label's real byte offset as it goes.
- For every short-form branch, computes the actual displacement to its target and flags it if it falls outside -128..127.
- Two parsing bugs were found and fixed along the way: (1) an early "unknown opcode" gate was intercepting `switch` before its dedicated multi-line-target-list handler could run, causing cascading false positives; (2) multi-line `ldstr bytearray (...)` hex-dump blocks weren't being skipped as a blob, so their raw hex bytes were sometimes mis-parsed as bogus standalone instructions. Both fixed by generalizing the existing directive-blob-skip logic to any instruction line with unbalanced parens in its operand text.

**Results.** With both parsing bugs fixed, a full run against the entire ~130,000-line source found exactly 10 out-of-range short branches across 7 methods — a real, complete, trustworthy list (0 false positives, 0 label-not-found noise):
- `CompanionRest.FindNearbyBed` — `brtrue.s` at 0x3e → `IL_00ba`, displacement 133 (the exact bug from the user's log).
- `CompanionSetup.CreateCompanionTombstone` — 3 instances (`brfalse.s` ×2, `brtrue.s` ×1, all targeting `KEEPSPLIT_DONE`).
- `CompanionRest.UpdateTombstoneRecovery` — `bne.un.s`, displacement 163.
- `CompanionRest.DirectSleep` — `brfalse.s`, displacement 153.
- `CompanionRest.FinalizeSleep` — `brfalse.s`, displacement 137.
- `CompanionRest.TryClaimBedIfNeeded` — 2 instances (`brfalse.s` ×2, both targeting `TCB_TRYFIND`).
- `HomesteadController.DoRepairBuildings` — `brtrue.s`, displacement 129. **Not reported by any user** — found purely by the full-codebase scan; unclear if/when this would have surfaced in play.

**Fix.** All 10 sites fixed the same way: dropped the `.s` suffix (e.g. `brtrue.s` → `brtrue`, `bne.un.s` → `bne.un`), converting each to its long form (4-byte displacement). `ilasm` recomputes the real offsets correctly once the mnemonic no longer forces a 1-byte encoding — no manual offset math needed for the fix itself, only for detection. Re-ran `check_branches.py` after the edits: **0 problems found across all 1605 methods** in the file.

**Build verification.** `ilasm` reassembled clean; `monodis --typedef` succeeds; `strings -e l` confirms the new build tag (`v51-branch-range-fix`) and version string (`Offline Companions v1.4.33 loading...`) are baked in; `verify_all.exe` JIT-sweep still reports `TOTAL=1553 FAILED=0` (expected — this harness doesn't catch this bug class either way, so a clean result here is not proof of the fix, just the existing baseline check). No in-game confirmation yet as of delivery.

**Not yet investigated (same log, separate issues):**
- `MissingMethodException: Method not found: single .ZInput.GetJoyLeftStickX()` in `CompanionRadialMenu.Update()` — likely relates to the user's own note that an Xbox controller "works, it's just a little bit wonky moving the cursor around."
- `EndOfStreamException` in `ZPackage.ReadBool()` during `SmeltController.UpdateInsertingItem`.

**Lesson reinforced.** This is the second class of bug in two rounds that passes every available "did it compile" check (`ilasm` clean, `monodis --typedef` clean, `verify_all.exe` JIT-sweep clean) and only crashes for real in the actual game. Both were caused by hand-inserting code near existing size/offset-dependent constructs (`.maxstack` counts, short-branch displacements) without manually re-deriving the now-changed value. `check_branches.py` should be kept in the toolkit and rerun (`python3 check_branches.py Companions_source.il`) after any future edit that inserts/removes code near an existing short-form branch, the same way `.maxstack` needs to be manually re-verified after any stack-depth-affecting edit.

Previous version: **1.4.31** — configurable auto-respawn timer layered on top of the existing manual tombstone resurrect. Dead companions now auto-respawn after a configurable delay (default 5 min) at their claimed bed, world spawn, or near their owner; they keep 100% of their inventory by default (with drop/drop-except-equipped toggles), and if inventory is dropped the tombstone stays lootable and the companion recovers it themselves once back within a configurable range (default 50m). Manual tombstone hold-resurrect is unchanged. A hotfix (build tag `v49b`) was needed after in-game testing found an `InvalidProgramException` from a `.maxstack` mismatch in `OnCompanionDeath` — fixed, and now always double-checked via the JIT-preparation sweep introduced this round.

Previous version: **1.4.30** — bed-claiming + no-bed complaint. Companions now skip beds a real player has personally claimed (`Bed.GetOwner() != 0`), a bed a companion is using shows "claimed by <name>" in its hover text (cosmetic-only Harmony postfix on `Bed.GetHoverText`, no real game ownership touched), and companions speak up (new `SpeechConfig.NoBed` line category) when they can't find a usable bed at night. Compiled clean via `ilasm`, untested in-game as of delivery.

Previous version: **1.4.29** — Valheim shipped a game patch (build 25527701, Sep 25 2026) that changed several API signatures the mod calls directly: `Inventory.AddItem(string,...)` gained a new trailing `bool pickedUp` param, `Piece.SetCreator(int64)` gained a new `Splatform.PlatformUserID` param, and the `VisEquipment.Set*Item` gear-visual methods (SetRightItem/SetLeftItem/SetChestItem/SetLegItem/SetHelmetItem/SetShoulderItem/SetUtilityItem) switched from taking the item's name (`string`) to a stable `int32` hash of it — matching the pattern the mod already used for SetHairItem/SetBeardItem. Found via Hexium's own compatibility checker on the published listing, confirmed against the current `assembly_valheim.dll`, and fixed at all 11 call sites (7 VisEquipment, 3 AddItem, 1 SetCreator). No behavior change — `pickedUp` passed as `false` (matches prior behavior), `PlatformUserID` passed as a default/empty struct (companions aren't platform accounts), item-name hashing uses the existing `StringExtensionMethods.GetStableHashCode` helper already used elsewhere in the mod. Compiled clean via `ilasm`, untested in-game as of delivery.

Previous version: **1.4.27** — fixed the real root cause of "direct-attack-ordered companion never swaps weapons / just holds up the bow": `DirectAttack` was never actually engaging the custom combat AI, so `SelectBestAttack` (weapon dispatch + shield + flee) never ran for a player-commanded attack. Also gave the flee threshold its own config entry (default 15%, separate from the shield-retreat threshold), let the companion pick a shield while fleeing, and added a "defend yourself if you can't escape, then keep trying to flee" fallback. Compiled clean via `ilasm`, untested in-game as of delivery.
- `Companions_helpers.cs` — C# source of the helper classes appended to the IL (GridDiag, GridFix, FontFix, WeaponLogic, EquipDiag) plus two throwaway stub classes at the top (CompanionsPlugin, ModConfig) that are NOT transplanted. Rebuild recipe: compile with mcs against the game's Managed DLLs + stub BepInEx/0Harmony assemblies, `MONO_PATH=<Managed> monodis` it, then replace everything from GridDiag's `.namespace Companions` block to EOF in the IL (rename `[0Harmony]`→`['0Harmony']`). Or just edit the IL directly.
- `Companions_source.il` — full IL source, ilasm-ready (`ilasm /dll /output=Companions.dll Companions_source.il`). **Needs the 27 embedded-resource PNGs alongside it** (the `.mresource` entries load them from files in the working dir): upload `Companions_resources.zip` (all 27 PNGs, unzip next to the .il before running ilasm). Fallback if the zip is missing: run `monodis` on any shipped Companions.dll in an empty folder — it writes the PNGs out.
- This notes file

## To resume in a new conversation
Also upload `Companions_resources.zip` (embedded PNGs needed by ilasm) and the log. `Game.il` (vanilla `assembly_valheim` disassembly, from the user's Managed folder) can be re-uploaded to skip re-disassembly; `InventoryGrid_UpdateGui.il` is just that one method.
Upload `Companions_source.il` and this file, and say you want to continue from here. No need to re-upload the original DLL or re-disassemble anything — just `ilasm` this file directly. You'll also want the reference assemblies (BepInEx.dll, 0Harmony.dll, assembly_valheim.dll, assembly_utils.dll, UnityEngine.CoreModule.dll, etc.) re-uploaded if further verification/compilation against them is needed.

## Fixed tonight (all confirmed compiling, most confirmed working in-game)
- `Character.Message` / `Game.SavePlayerProfile` / two `SEMan.AddStatusEffect` overloads / `EffectList.Create` — all gained a new trailing parameter in Valheim 1.0; fixed by appending the correct default value (verified against real vanilla call sites, not guessed).
- `VisEquipment.SetHairItem` / `SetBeardItem` — changed from `(string)` to `(int32)`, now hash the string first via `StringExtensionMethods.GetStableHashCode`, matching vanilla.
- `ZInput.GetJoyLeftStickX/Y`, `GetJoyRightStickX/Y` — moved to `assembly_utils`, now static/parameterless (was instance+bool).
- `CompanionPrefabs.CreateCompanionPrefab`'s call to `CompanionSkills.InitSkillDefs` — wrapped in try/catch so a failure there can't cascade into breaking the whole prefab setup (root cause was a generic-method IL round-trip issue in the `ilasm`/`monodis` toolchain, not a real Valheim API change; the try/catch contains it rather than needing a deeper fix).
- `CompanionPrefabs.DestroyComponent<T>` — rewritten from a generic method to a `Type`-parameter version (same toolchain generic-method issue as above).
- `InventoryGrid.SetSelection` — removed in 1.0, split into `SetGamepadSelection` and `SetTouchSelection`; now calls both (user confirmed they swap between mouse and gamepad).
- Weapon auto-equip AI (`Companions.CombatPatches.EquipBestWeapon_PreferHighestDamage`) — rewritten to score candidates by *effective* damage against the current target's live resistances/weaknesses (`Character.GetDamageModifiers` + a modifier→multiplier lookup), not raw summed damage. Added a 10% hysteresis margin so it won't flip-flop between near-tied weapons.
- **The long-running "tooltip placeholder" / empty-inventory saga** — root cause was the companion's custom `CompanionInteractPanel` cloning `InventoryGui`'s own container panel as a UI template, then never fully replicating several fields Valheim's real `InventoryGrid.UpdateGui` depends on (`m_elementPrefab`, `m_dropFocusFader`, `m_elements`). Fixed by copying/initializing these onto the clone. Also found and removed a *separate* mechanism (`InjectGamepadGroups`) that was redirecting vanilla's own `InventoryGui.m_containerGrid` at the (broken) clone — that redirect is gone now, vanilla's real grid is left alone.
- Ownership race: `CompanionInteract.OpenCompanionInventory` calls `ZNetView.ClaimOwnership()` (async) then immediately `Show()`s the UI without waiting for the transfer to complete — real chests avoid this via an RPC round-trip (`RPC_RequestOpen` → `RPC_OpenResponse`) before ever calling `Show()`. Fixed pragmatically: if not already owner, claim ownership, log a message, and return without opening — user just interacts again a moment later once ownership has transferred.
- **Most recent fix (1.3.9, untested as of last message):** the panel's `_root`/`_grid` GameObject is built once and *reused* across opens (there's a `_built` flag gating rebuilds). The three field-fixes above were only being applied during that one-time build, so they didn't survive whatever happens to the grid's state across a close/reopen cycle — matching the repeated "works a few times, then tooltip garbage again" pattern. Fix: the same field-repair logic now also runs at the top of `CompanionInteractPanel.Show()`, unconditionally, every single open — not just the first build.

## Known but NOT yet fixed / not chased down
- `InventoryGrid.UpdateContainer` is Harmony-patched by `Companions.CompanionInteractPanel+InventoryGuiUpdateContainerPatch.Prefix` — now returns `true` (lets vanilla's real update run) instead of the original `false`; this was necessary for the grid-visibility fix but hasn't been stress-tested for edge cases (e.g. switching between multiple different open containers rapidly).
- ZenUI (a separate installed mod) has its own confirmed real bug — `ZenUI.Section.InventoryStackAmountText`'s Harmony transpiler on `InventoryGrid.UpdateGui` calls `unbox.any Int32` on a possibly-null boxed argument, no null-check. This is a bug in ZenUI itself, not Companions.dll. Not fixed — out of scope unless the user wants ZenUI patched too. `Zen.ModLib.dll` and `Jotunn.dll` were obtained as dependencies if this gets picked up again.
- `CompanionFood.ConsumeIntoSlot` was read through but not fully audited end-to-end for the "raspberry consumption" report — that lead was abandoned once the empty-container-also-crashes evidence ruled out an item-data theory. Probably fine, not re-verified.

## Toolchain / methodology notes for continuing
- Environment: `monodis`/`ilasm` from `mono-complete` (apt). This toolchain has known quirks: generic method calls sometimes fail to round-trip correctly (see `InitSkillDefs`/`DestroyComponent<T>` above) and it can produce a broken `bool::ToString()` encoding (a raw-primitive `TypeSpec`) that *prints identically* to the correct `TypeRef`-based encoding in `monodis` text output but doesn't run — if writing new `ToString()` calls on value types, always call through `ldloca` + `valuetype [mscorlib]System.Boolean` (or whichever type) explicitly, and verify by inspecting the compiled MemberRef/TypeSpec token directly (via `dnfile`), not by comparing disassembled text against "working" examples.
- `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` via reflection (see `fullverify.cs` pattern used throughout) is a good but *not fully reliable* substitute for Valheim's real runtime verifier — it has missed at least two real bugs tonight that only showed up in-game. Treat a clean PrepareMethod pass as necessary, not sufficient.
- Every vanilla signature change fixed tonight was verified against real vanilla call sites in the disassembled game assembly (`assembly_valheim.dll` → `Game.il`), not guessed — this is why the fixes have mostly held up. Keep doing this for anything new.
- Version is bumped by hand-editing the `BepInPlugin` custom-attribute blob bytes near the top of the IL file (search for the version string, e.g. `33 2E 39` = "3.9") — same-length version strings are a trivial single-byte edit; longer ones need the length-prefix byte updated too.

## 1.4.0 changes (diagnostic build)
- **Font repair disabled**: `CompanionsPlugin.OnSceneLoaded` is now a bare `ret`. `EnsureTmpDefaultFont`/`FindReplacementTmpFont`/`IsBrokenTmpFont` still exist but are dead code. (The old error: one `set_font` threw inside the FindObjectsOfTypeAll loop, aborting the repair partway.) If companion UI text looks wrong, the plan is to set the font explicitly on texts the panel creates, copied from a vanilla TMP_Text.
- **Inventory bug diagnosis** (tooltip-placeholder slots after adding an item to the NPC): log shows ~996 NREs from `InventoryGrid.UpdateGui` via `CompanionInteractPanel.UpdateGrid`. ZenUI is NOT loaded and not the cause. Errors start only once an item exists in the container (opens with `items=0` were clean), so the null is in UpdateGui's per-item section (element children like m_icon/m_equiped/m_queued/m_noteleport/m_food/m_durability/m_quality/m_amount/m_tooltip, or the ItemData). `m_uiGroup` ruled out (unguarded reads occur before the per-item loop and passed on empty opens). Vanilla passes `null` player for container grids, so the null-player-on-first-call is normal.
- `UpdateGrid` now wraps `UpdateInventory` in try/catch → `Companions.GridDiag.Report(ex, grid, inventory, player, dragItem)`; `GridDiag.Ok()` on success resets the failure streak. First 2 faults per streak log `[GRIDDIAG]` warnings: exception text + top-frame IL offset (map to `InventoryGrid_UpdateGui.il`), inventory/first-3-item details, null-field dump of the grid, element[0], the first item's slot element, the element prefab (+ whether its `binding` child exists), and Harmony patch owners of UpdateGui. Further faults are suppressed (counts at 100/1000/10000). Exceptions are swallowed; on fault the rest of UpdateGrid (gridCreated flag, weight/armor, food slots) is skipped, same as before.
- `[BUILD]` tag is `v17-griddiag-fontoff`; BepInPlugin version bytes are "1.4.0".
- `GridDiag` is written in C# (non-generic on purpose), compiled with mcs against the Managed DLLs + stub BepInEx/0Harmony, disassembled with `MONO_PATH=<Managed> monodis`, and appended to the IL source (rename `[0Harmony]` → `['0Harmony']`, all refs come out as `[mscorlib]`).
- Other oddities noted, not investigated: `HideInternal reason=ContainerSwitch` fires right after every Show; `Gamepad injected` still logs (InjectGamepadGroups still runs).
- Verified: ilasm OK, `PrepareMethod` OK for all GridDiag/CompanionInteractPanel methods, exception clauses inspected. Not yet run in-game.

## 1.4.1 — ROOT CAUSE of the NPC-inventory "tooltip"/uninteractable-slots bug (found via GRIDDIAG)
- Log (clean install, only this mod; `Harmony.GetPatchInfo` on UpdateGui = no patches) showed NRE in vanilla `InventoryGrid.UpdateGui` at IL offset 0x073B = `element.m_canBeDroppedOn = CanDropDragOntoItem(item)`: an **unguarded call through the grid's `Func<ItemData,bool> CanDropDragOntoItem` field, which was null on our cloned grid**. Vanilla `InventoryGui.Awake` assigns it (via Delegate.Combine to `InventoryGui.CanDropDragOntoItem`, a private method) on m_playerGrid/m_containerGrid only. It runs per item, so empty opens were fine and errors began once an item existed; UpdateGui aborted for the slot so icons/stack/tooltips never populated. Other unset delegates (m_onReleased, m_onEnter, OnMoveToLowerInventoryGrid, OnSetTouchSelection) are null-guarded in vanilla — harmless. Earlier suspicion of other mods (BetterUI/Jewelcrafting) and ZenUI was wrong; the user's clean-install test was right.
- Fix: new `Companions.GridFix.EnsureCanDrop(InventoryGrid)` (C# → IL, like GridDiag) creates the delegate by reflection on `InventoryGui.instance` for the private vanilla method and assigns it if the field is null. Called at the top of the try block in `UpdateGrid` on every call (self-healing). Logs `[GridFix] Bound vanilla ...` once.
- GridDiag catch is still in (first 2 faults per streak logged, rest suppressed). Build tag `v18-candrop-fix`, BepInPlugin "1.4.1".
- Transplant script: C# helpers live in one file (GridDiag + GridFix); everything from GridDiag's `.namespace` block to EOF is replaced in the IL each build; rename `[0Harmony]`→`['0Harmony']`.
- Still-open observations: `m_hasTouchSelected=True` on our grid (from our SetTouchSelection replacement of the removed SetSelection; abnormal for mouse); `HideInternal reason=ContainerSwitch` right after every Show; `Gamepad injected` still logs. Untested: whether item transfer/interaction works now that UpdateGui runs fully.

## 1.4.2 — weapon logic, font fix, equip diagnostics (UNTESTED)
Decisions from the user: ordinary axes (stone/flint/etc.) ARE weapons; only pickaxes and true tools (hammer, cultivator, fishing rod = ItemType Tool / pickaxe damage / fishing animation) are excluded. Out of combat: best weapon by score; in combat: same score with the target's damage-type weaknesses (Weak 1.5, VeryWeak 2, SlightlyWeak 1.25, Resistant 0.5, VeryResistant 0.25, SlightlyResistant 0.75, Immune/Ignore 0). No weapon -> unarmed (+ unbroken shield; shield selection is independent in AutoEquipBest). 10% switch margin exposed as config `Combat/WeaponSwitchMargin` (F8 panel, default 0.10, range 0-0.5).
- `WeaponLogic.Score(item, target)` = damage per hit (x enemy multipliers when target given) x speed tier: Fast 1.25 (Knives, Spears, Unarmed skill), Slow 0.75 (two-handed Axes/Clubs: battleaxe, sledge), else 1.0. Tiers are guesses from a dated community sheet (first-hit ~1.1s sword/axe, 0.6-0.7 dagger/spear, 1.6 battleaxe, 2.1 sledge). `SwitchFactor()` = 1 + margin.
- Now used by: `CompanionSetup.AutoEquipBest` (replaced its raw 9-field damage sum with Score(item,null)); `CombatPatches.EquipBestWeapon_PreferHighestDamage` (GetEffectiveCombatDamage/GetCombatDamage bodies -> Score; hysteresis constant 1.1 -> SwitchFactor()); `CompanionCombatAI.GetCombatDamage` (-> Score(item,null)). TryForceMeleeWeapon already excludes pickaxes and pure-chop tools but allows axes (slash>0).
- **Equip loop (unresolved root cause)**: debug log showed (a) axe-only: queue logs `equipped axe` but next pass (from OnInventoryChanged) sees `current="none"`, alternating with `1H="none"`; EquipItem's return value was being discarded; (b) club->axe: `SyncEquipmentToInventory` (9 callers) re-runs AutoEquipBest which calls ClearEquipQueue first -> pending equip dropped, club stays current. It predates the scoring rewrite. 1.4.2 adds a guard: AutoEquipBest returns immediately if `_equipQueue.Count > 0` (fixes (b) presumably), and `EquipDiag` (installed manually after PatchAll via reflection, so a bad patch can't break PatchAll) logs at Info level with `[EQUIPDIAG]`: Humanoid.EquipItem result + m_rightItem/m_hiddenRightItem + caller stack, UnequipItem/HideHandItems/ShowHandItems callers (first 40-60 each), and an inventory dump whenever AutoEquipBest finds no 1H weapon. Vanilla 1.0 has `m_hiddenRightItem` (sheathed weapon), a prime suspect for `current="none"`. Note vanilla EquipItem returns false if IsItemEquiped, not in inventory, InAttack/InDodge, or broken.
- **Font**: `OnSceneLoaded` now calls `FontFix.Ensure` which only sets `TMP_Settings.defaultFontAsset` to a valid font (prefers names containing averia/norse/valheim) — no per-text loop (that loop's `set_font` was what threw). Fixes blank overhead bar texts (Name/HPText/STAText).
- Enable Debug logs for more detail: BepInEx.cfg `[Logging.Disk] LogLevels = All`.
- Not changed: explicit unequip of a tool when no weapon exists (relies on vanilla AI's CanUseAttack exclusion for unarmed fallback) — verify in test.

## 1.4.3 — ROOT CAUSE of the equip/unequip loop (found via [EQUIPDIAG] in the 1.4.2 log)
- Trace: `EquipItem(axe) -> true` (right=axe) is followed immediately by `Container.Load -> Inventory.Load -> Inventory.Changed -> CompanionSetup.OnInventoryChanged -> UnequipMissingEquippedItems -> UnequipIfMissing -> UnequipItem(axe)`. At that moment the inventory dump shows only the mushroom (`items=1`): the axe hasn't been re-added yet. So: vanilla 1.0 `Container.Load()` reloads whenever the ZDO **DataRevision** differs from `m_lastRevision` (any write on the shared ZDO — equipment hashes, food, stamina, etc. — not just the items byte array), recreating every ItemData; `Inventory.Changed` fires mid-load with a partial inventory; our handler then unequips gear that is merely "not re-added yet"; AutoEquipBest re-equips -> ZDO write -> reload -> loop. Affects every equipped slot (armor too), not just axes. Predates scoring changes. (Scoring itself worked: with an iron sledge given, `desired="$item_sledge_iron"` was chosen.)
- Fix (`ContainerFix` in helpers, Harmony patches installed manually via reflection from `EquipDiag.Install`, so failures can't break PatchAll): prefix on private `Container.Load()` for companion containers (Container + Humanoid on the same GameObject): if this client OWNS the ZNetView, the container was previously loaded/saved as owner, and the ZDO `s_items` bytes equal the last loaded/saved bytes, skip the reload (set `__result=false`, update `m_lastRevision`). Postfix on `Load()` (when it returned true) and `Save()` record owner flag + items bytes. External changes to the items bytes, or a non-owner -> owner transition, still reload. Logs `[ContainerFix] installed.` and once `Skipping redundant Container.Load...`.
- Still true: UnequipMissingEquippedItems isn't guarded against mid-load partial inventories (now only happens on spawn/ownership change).
- Overhead bar values (HPText/STAText over the NPC's head): font warnings are gone (default set to Valheim-AveriaSansLibre) but the numbers still don't show on the bars over the head (they do in the minimap panel). Probably the mod never fills those vanilla 1.0 texts in `EnemyHudPatches`; needs a look at CreateBar/the per-frame update. Low priority.
- `EquipDiag` tracing is still on (rate-limited, Info level) — remove once the loop is confirmed fixed.
- Build recipe note: compiling helpers now also needs `-r:SoftReferenceableAssets.dll` (assembly_valheim depends on it).

## 1.4.4 — Torch opener ("ignite then swap") — UNTESTED
User request: companions should use a torch against enemies weak to fire to set them alight, then swap to the best weapon.
- `TorchOpener` (helpers.cs, per-humanoid state in Hashtables). `TrySelect(humanoid, target, stance, huntMode)` is called from `CompanionCombatAI.SelectBestAttack` right after `_updateWeaponTimer = 1.0` (i.e. ~1/s while engaged and not in an attack); `true` => `br IL_024a` (skip normal weapon selection). Start conditions: `UseTorchOpener` on; not hunt mode; stance Balanced(0) or Melee(3); own HP% >= `HealthRetreatPct`; target `GetMaxHealth() >= TorchOpenerMinTargetHealth`; target's Fire modifier is Weak/VeryWeak/SlightlyWeak; target not already Burning and not Wet (SEMan.s_statusEffectBurning/Wet); no cooldown on that target; a usable torch (ItemType.Torch, durability>0) in inventory; another usable melee weapon in inventory (1H/2H/2H-left, aiAttackRange<=10, not a pickaxe); torch not already in the right hand. Action: `Humanoid.EquipItem(torch, true)` (vanilla path unequips the right item; may also drop a shield -- AutoEquipBest re-equips it afterwards).
- While igniting: `IsActive()` makes `CompanionSetup.AutoEquipBest` return immediately (guard inserted before the queue guard); `Tick()` is called every frame from `UpdateEquipQueue` and ends the opener on: target burning, timeout (`TorchOpenerTimeout`), target dead/changed, torch no longer in hand/unusable, or low HP. `End()` sets the per-target cooldown (`TorchOpenerCooldown`) and calls `CompanionSetup.AutoEquipBest` by reflection so the best weapon is re-equipped immediately. All `[TorchOpener]` messages log at Info.
- New config (Combat category, F8 panel): `UseTorchOpener` (true), `TorchOpenerTimeout` (4s, 1-10), `TorchOpenerCooldown` (10s, 0-60), `TorchOpenerMinTargetHealth` (40, 0-500).
- Facts learned: vanilla `GetCurrentWeapon()` returns the right item if it is a weapon, else the LEFT item unless it is a torch (ItemType 15), else unarmed -> a left-hand torch is only a light; the opener needs the torch in the RIGHT hand. Vanilla EquipItem torch path: right item null/left item present -> jumps to the path that unequips the right item and possibly the left item depending on its type.
- Unverified: whether the torch's AI attack range/`CanUseAttack` lets the companion actually swing it, how often a torch hit applies Burning, whether a shield is dropped during the opener, and the wait `readyAt` (0.4s) is currently unused. Stamina retreat is not checked (companion stamina is a custom system); only HP.
- Remaining cleanup queue: remove `EquipDiag` tracing and the old `[DIAG] Reached end of grid setup` warning; overhead bar HP/STA numbers; tune speed tiers.

## 1.4.5 — starter spawn fix + Balanced distance logic (UNTESTED)
- **Starter spawn bug**: `CompanionManager.SpawnStarterCompanion` set `Player.m_customData["HC_StarterCompanion_<worldUID>"]="1"` and saved the profile when the panel was SHOWN, so closing the panel/quitting early left the flag set and the panel never showed again. Fix: removed that set+save from the panel-show path (the "found existing companion in scene" branch still sets it), and `SpawnStarterWithAppearance` now calls `StarterFix.MarkSpawned()` after `SpawnCompanion` returns true. New config `General/ResetStarterFlag` (bool, default false): when true at world load, `StarterFix.ResetIfRequested()` removes the flag for the current world, saves the profile and sets the toggle back to false (needed once for characters already wrongly flagged).
- **Vanilla facts**: `Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon` is distance/range based (skips weapons if distance < `m_aiAttackRangeMin`, considers `m_aiAttackRange`, attack interval, and finally picks RANDOMLY among eligible weapons); `BaseAI.CanUseAttack` has no ammo/durability check. The old damage-rank postfix ignored distance and included bows (latent conflict).
- **Balanced only** (stance 0 and not HuntMode; Melee/Ranged/Passive untouched): new Harmony `Prefix` on `EquipBestWeapon` -> `WeaponLogic.BalancedPrefix(h)`; returns false (skips vanilla) when it made the decision. Logic: distance = centre distance - target radius; candidates = IsWeapon && Ready(durability) && CanUseAttack && aiTargetType==Enemy; bows (ItemType.Bow or non-empty `m_ammoType`) need ammo (`Inventory.GetAmmoItem(ammoType,null)`); want ranged if a usable bow+ammo exists and dist > `BowSwitchDistance` (20), want melee if dist < `MeleeSwitchDistance` (12), keep current class in between; no bow/ammo -> melee (this is the arrows-run-out fallback); within a class, best `Score` vs target with `SwitchFactor()` margin; class change ignores the margin. Logs `[Balanced] dist=.. -> item (ranged|melee)` (max 60). The old postfix now returns immediately for Balanced companions (`IsBalancedCompanion` guard) and is unchanged for other stances. `BowSwitchDistance` and `MeleeSwitchDistance` config entries are now actually read.
- **Findings for later**: `TryForceRangedWeapon` (Ranged stance) equips the first ItemType.Bow found with NO arrow or durability check (page claims otherwise; only arrow-related text in the mod is the speech "I need a bow and arrows to shoot!"); its return value is ignored in the ranged path, so no bow -> vanilla's melee pick stands. 27 config entries are never read by any code (StaminaRetreatPct, HealthRecoverPct, StaminaRecoverPct, PowerAttackCooldown, BowMaxRange, BowMinRange, [BowSwitchDistance, MeleeSwitchDistance now wired], RetreatDistance, BowDrawTime, BowFireInterval, DodgeCooldown, DodgeStaminaCost, ThreatDetectRange, ProjectileDetectRange, BlockSafetyCap, CounterWindowDuration, ConsumeCooldown, UpdateTargetIntervalNear/Far, SelfDefenseRange, ViewRange, ViewAngle, HomesteadScanInterval/TaskSlotTime/SmeltSlotTime/ScanBackoff); many of their defaults appear as hard-coded literals in CompanionCombatAI (e.g. 0.15 stamina retreat, 0.5 health recover, 3.0 power-attack cooldown, 20/10 bow distances), so the shipped DLL simply hard-codes them (planned-but-unwired config, not something our patches removed).
- **Agreed roadmap (do 1-2 items at a time)**: (1) starter spawn fix [done 1.4.5] (2) Balanced distance guard [done 1.4.5] (3) overhead bar settings: show/hide bars, show/hide values/titles (HP, STAM) + fix missing numbers over head (4) shield policy: default = best parry shield (`m_timedBlockBonus`), swap to highest `m_blockPower` (tower) when health low OR multiple attackers, swap back with hysteresis; runtime stats not names; also make torch opener pick the BEST torch (highest fire damage) instead of the first usable one (5) Ranged fallback to melee (no bow/broken bow/no arrows; enemy inside min range for a couple of seconds; return to bow when distance restored) + Passive flee (if set to follow -> run to player; if working/not following -> run to home; stop when clear) (6) night light (torch left hand at night while foraging/gathering, needs 1H right item; toggle) (7) wire or remove the 27 dead config entries. Server-only question deferred.

## 1.4.6 — Balanced weapon-swap patch wasn't actually running (found from the 1.4.5 log)
- User confirmed the companion WAS in Balanced stance, yet the log showed the bow equipped via plain `Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon` (vanilla) with zero `[Balanced]` log lines anywhere in the whole session — proof `WeaponLogic.BalancedPrefix` never ran (or always hit an early `return true`), so vanilla's own random/range-based pick (no ammo check) is what the user was seeing, explaining the still-swapping-with-no-arrows symptom.
- Root cause candidate: the 1.4.5 `Prefix`/`Postfix` methods on `EquipBestWeapon_PreferHighestDamage` had NO explicit `[HarmonyPrefix]`/`[HarmonyPostfix]` custom attribute — relying on Harmony's name-based auto-detection. Everywhere else in this codebase (confirmed by grep) explicit attributes are used for every other prefix/postfix; this one method was the exception. Whether that's why it silently no-op'd is unconfirmed (could also be a genuine early-return in `BalancedPrefix`), but there was also literally no log evidence either way since `BalLog` only fires on an actual swap, not on "ran, decided no change needed."
- Fix: added explicit `.custom instance void class ['0Harmony']HarmonyLib.HarmonyPrefix::'.ctor'()` / `HarmonyPostfix::'.ctor'()` attributes to both methods (matching the pattern used by every other patch in the mod). Also added a rate-limited (40) entry/skip log inside `BalancedPrefix` — `[Balanced] skip: <reason>` or `[Balanced] handling weapon choice for this call.` — so the NEXT log will show definitively whether the prefix is being invoked at all, and if so, which early-exit branch (not-Balanced / torch-active / no-target / target-dead/null / no-weapon-or-inventory) it's hitting when it doesn't swap.
- Build tag `v23-balanced-diag-explicit-attrs`, version 1.4.6.
- Kiting large monsters: user wants to revisit later — NOT implemented, deferred at user's request. Water-chase/drowning avoidance is also still just a proposal, not built (bundled with kiting when we return to it).
- Still open: whether the ammo check needs to extend beyond Balanced into the Melee/Ranged scoring path (`WeaponLogic.Score` has no ammo awareness) — revisit once Balanced is confirmed working, likely alongside the Ranged-fallback item.

## 1.4.7 — Balanced diagnostics deepened + water safety (UNTESTED)
- **Balanced swap still not engaging (found from 1.4.6 log)**: the explicit-attribute fix DID work (Harmony now calls our Prefix — 40 `[Balanced]` log lines appeared, vs zero before), but 36/40 said `skip: not a Balanced companion (stance/hunt/type check failed)` even though the user confirmed the companion WAS in Balanced — so `IsBalancedCompanion`/`IsBalanced` has a real bug in one of its 3 sub-checks (AI type name, has-CompanionCombatAI-component, stance value), which the old log couldn't distinguish. `WeaponLogic.LastReason` now records exactly which sub-check failed and why (e.g. "BaseAI type is X, not CompanionAI", "no CompanionCombatAI component", "stance=N (0=Balanced) hunt=bool", "reflection could not find field _stance"), surfaced via the existing `[Balanced] skip: ...` log line. Symptom this explains: bow equipped well inside melee range, "randomly" — that's exactly vanilla `Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon`'s own behavior (range-gated eligibility list, then `Random.Range` pick, no ammo check) running unguarded because our prefix keeps deferring to it.
- **Water safety, two independent pieces** (`WaterSafety` in helpers.cs):
  1. **Won't willingly start a chase into water**: `ShouldAvoidChase(self, target)` — false if the companion is already swimming (any reason, doesn't block an in-progress crossing or following the player), else true if the target `IsSwimming()`. Hooked as an early return-false at the very top of `CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat` (the single method that contains all 3 of the class's `MoveToPoint` calls toward a target). Returning false there is the same graceful "nothing to do in combat this tick" path the method already uses when it has no target — companion falls through to normal follow-the-owner logic, and vanilla's own target-forget timer clears the target if the player doesn't re-approach. Not tested whether this seam is quite right (e.g. whether it lets the companion still fight if already adjacent to water's edge but target is just past the shoreline) — first pass.
  2. **Low-stamina shore-seeking**: `WaterSafety.Tick(BaseAI, dt)` hooked into `CompanionAI.UpdateAI` right after the `base.UpdateAI(dt)` call succeeds; if it takes over movement this tick, `UpdateAI` returns `true` immediately (skipping follow/combat/gather for that tick only — no persistent state changed, so whatever it was doing resumes on its own next tick once done). Trigger: swimming (`Character.IsSwimming()`) AND (already seeking, OR `CompanionStamina.Stamina/MaxStamina` <= new config `WaterStaminaFleeThreshold`, default 0.25, F8 Combat category, range 0-1). Once triggered it keeps heading for shore until `IsSwimming()` goes false (reached land), regardless of stamina recovering mid-swim, to avoid flapping. Shore point: samples 10 points per ring at 10/20/30/40/50m via `ZoneSystem.instance.GetGroundHeight(point, out height)`, accepts the first ring with any point at or above the water level (`Character.GetLiquidLevel()`) minus 0.3m, picks the nearest sample in that ring; re-searches every 3s while still seeking. Movement executes via reflection on `CompanionAI.MoveToPoint(dt, point, dist, run)` (an `assembly`/internal method) since the C# helper can't reference the in-progress Companions.dll directly.
  - Bug caught before building: `CompanionAI` is a `BaseAI` (attached to the same GameObject as, but NOT the same object as, the `Humanoid`) — an early version of `Tick` wrongly took a `Humanoid` param and the IL passed `this` (CompanionAI) for it, which would have failed to verify/load. Fixed by having `Tick` take only `BaseAI` and pull the actual `Character` off `BaseAI`'s protected `m_character` field via reflection (no public accessor exists on `BaseAI` itself, `GetCharacter()` belongs to `Sadle`, not `BaseAI` — caught by checking Game.il, not by trial and error).
  - Not implemented: kiting large monsters (still deferred at user's request).
  - Build tag `v24-water-safety-balanced-diag2`, version 1.4.7. `PrepareMethod` OK for 354 methods across all changed classes; `UpdateCombat` and `UpdateAI` reflected back and confirmed their IL grew by exactly the inserted bytes with exception-handling clauses intact.

## 1.4.7 rebuild — fixed a real bug the verification process missed
- User reported companions (described as "my character... sliding around like falling") were broken after the first 1.4.7 build. Log showed 1542x `InvalidProgramException: Invalid IL code in Companions.CompanionAI:UpdateAI (single): IL_0016: ret` — the game's own Mono runtime rejected the method's IL outright, so `UpdateAI` never ran at all for any companion, every tick.
- Root cause: the `WaterSafety.Tick` hook was inserted AFTER the pre-existing `IL_000b: ldarg.0` instead of before it. That left an unconsumed `this` reference sitting on the evaluation stack; on the early-return path (`ldc.i4.1 / ret`), the stack held two values (`[this, 1]`) instead of the one value `ret` requires for a `bool`-returning method — invalid IL, rejected by the verifier at JIT time.
- **This was NOT caught by `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod`** (reported "prepared ok" on the broken build) — PrepareMethod is not a reliable substitute for full IL verification, consistent with the caution already in these notes about it. Fixed by moving the insertion to occur at the `IL_000b:` label itself (empty stack guaranteed, since it's a branch target with no other predecessors), so both exit paths of the inserted block are stack-balanced.
- New, stronger verification step added to the process (used for this fix and worth repeating for future IL surgery near existing labels/branches): actually **invoke** the modified method via reflection on an uninitialized instance (`FormatterServices.GetUninitializedObject`), wrapped in try/catch. An `InvalidProgramException` means the IL itself is still bad; any other exception (here, `TypeInitializationException` from Unity-only static setup code that can't run outside the engine) means the IL passed verification and real execution began. The broken build would have failed this test instantly; the fixed build gets past verification into actual method execution.
- No other changes from the previous entry — same Balanced-diagnostics-deepening and water-safety design as before, just with the stack bug fixed. Build tag `v25-water-safety-fix-stack-bug`, still version 1.4.7. Untested in-game as of this rebuild.

## 1.4.8 — "stuck on Passive after death" — ROOT CAUSE + fix
- 1.4.7 log: torch opener working as designed (ignite/timeout/target-changed all fired correctly). Player died and respawned; companion then ignored every stance change via the radial menu, staying on Passive.
- Root cause: `CompanionRadialMenu.ExecuteSelectedAction` calls `EnsureOwnership()` (which fires `ZNetView.ClaimOwnership()` if not already owner) and, on the SAME call, immediately calls `CompanionSetup.SetCombatStance()` — which writes straight to the ZDO with no ownership check of its own. `ClaimOwnership()` is not instant (RPC round-trip), so right after a death/respawn (when ownership legitimately needs to be re-established) the stance write happens before the claim completes and gets silently dropped/overwritten by the next sync from the actual owner. Exact same bug class as the original "ownership race" already fixed for the inventory panel (see the very first entries in this file) — just never patched here, because this code path wasn't exercised by that fix.
- Fix, in `CompanionRadialMenu` directly (IL edit, not a helper class — this needed access to the menu's own private fields):
  - New field `_pendingStance` (int, -1 = none), reset to -1 in `Show()`.
  - `ExecuteSelectedAction()`'s stance-setting branch now checks `_companionNview.IsOwner()` first: if owner, sets the stance immediately as before; if not, stores the desired stance in `_pendingStance` instead (and still calls `EnsureOwnership()` beforehand, unchanged).
  - `Update()` (runs every frame while the menu is open) now checks each frame: if `_pendingStance != -1` and ownership has since been confirmed, apply it via `SetCombatStance` and clear the pending flag.
- **Two real bugs of my own caught and fixed before this ever reached the user**, both in this round of IL surgery on `CompanionRadialMenu` (all three touched methods — `Show`, `Update`, `ExecuteSelectedAction` — were re-verified afterward):
  1. Stack-imbalance in an unrelated `CompanionAI.UpdateAI` edit from the *previous* (1.4.7) attempt — caused 1500+ `InvalidProgramException`s in-game, companions effectively frozen. Cause: new code was inserted *after* an existing instruction that already pushed a value onto the stack, leaving it unconsumed on one exit path. Fixed by moving the insertion to start exactly at the branch-target label instead.
  2. A second, different bug in *this* round: in both `Show()` and `Update()`, new code was inserted between an existing branch instruction and the label it already jumps to (e.g. `brfalse.s IL_000a`) — but the `IL_000a:` text stayed attached to the *original* instruction, so the branch jumped clean over the new code (not a crash — just silently dead code that would never execute). Fixed by moving the label text onto the first new instruction in each case.
  - **Verification approach that finally caught both classes reliably**: `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` was confirmed NOT sufficient (reported "ok" on the broken 1.4.7 build). Reflection-based direct invocation helped for the first bug but Unity's native bootstrap (UnityEngine.Object cctor etc.) blocks it for anything touching Unity types outside the real engine. What actually worked for both: disassembling the real compiled DLL (not trusting the source `.il` text) and manually tracing stack depth instruction-by-instruction from real branch targets through to their real landing points, confirming every merge point has consistent stack depth and that branches land where intended. `mono --verify-all` was attempted as a faster path to the same guarantee but hit unrelated sandbox assembly-resolution issues (missing/conflicting UnityEngine.CoreModule under that specific mono invocation mode) and was abandoned in favor of the manual trace.
- Build tag `v26-radial-stance-ownership-race`, version 1.4.8.

## 1.4.9 — diagnostic budget was getting starved by nearby monsters, not fixed yet
- User confirmed 1.4.8's water-safety (stays out of water, re-engages after death) and the ownership-race fix both work. Bow-without-arrows and "very slow to attack" persist.
- 1.4.8 log showed WHY we still have no visibility: `Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon` is a shared vanilla method, so the Harmony prefix fires for every nearby Humanoid enemy too (skeletons, goblins, draugr, etc.), not just the companion. 34 of the 40-line log budget were `skip: BaseAI type is MonsterAI, not CompanionAI` — monster noise correctly declining, but it exhausted the shared counter before the companion's own (still-failing) attempts could log anything useful. Only 6 lines were genuinely companion-relevant (`no target`), none showing the actual failure.
- Fix (logging only, no behavior change, no IL edits to existing game methods — just `GridDiag.cs`'s own `EntryLog`/`NonCompanionLog`): split into two counters. Non-companion skips (wrong AI type) now cap at 3 lines total for the whole session. The companion-relevant budget (has-component/stance/hunt-mode checks, no-target, torch-active, no-weapon-or-inventory, and — the one we actually need — "handling weapon choice for this call") raised from 40 to 200.
- The underlying bow-without-arrows / slow-to-attack bug is still unsolved. Likely candidate: since vanilla `Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon` is confirmed still running unguarded for the real companion (stack trace: `EquipItem < EquipBestWeapon < SelectBestAttack`, 18 equips + 9 unequips in the last log), repeated weapon swaps mid-fight would each cost an equip animation before the companion can land a hit — which would explain "slow to attack" as a symptom of the SAME root cause rather than a separate bug. Not confirmed; next log should show which `[Balanced] skip: ...` reason is actually firing for the companion, or whether it's reaching `handling weapon choice for this call` and something else entirely is wrong.
- Build tag `v27-balanced-log-budget-split`, version 1.4.9. Lower-risk change than the last two builds: no new insertion points into existing game-method IL, so the stack-balance/label-skip bug classes from 1.4.7/1.4.8 don't apply here. Verified via `PrepareMethod` (355/355) and resource byte match; did not repeat the full manual bytecode trace since no game-method bytecode was touched.

## 1.4.10 — the actual Balanced-swap root cause, plus a self-inflicted version-string bug
- 1.4.9's split log budget worked and gave a clean signal: 31/34 lines were `skip: no target`, and log timestamps showed this firing in the EXACT same instant the companion was actively swapping combat weapons (torch/axe unequip, bow equip, all via vanilla `EquipBestWeapon`, all immediately preceded by `[Balanced] skip: no target.`).
- **Root cause, confirmed**: `WeaponLogic.BalancedPrefix` was checking `ai.HaveTarget()` / `ai.GetTargetCreature()` — vanilla `BaseAI`'s own targeting state. But `CompanionCombatAI` keeps its OWN separate private field, `_targetCreature`, and evidently never syncs it into vanilla's `BaseAI` target tracking. So `ai.HaveTarget()` was structurally never going to see what the companion was actually fighting — this bug has been present since 1.4.5 when the Balanced logic was first added, just masked earlier by the ownership-race and log-budget bugs that got fixed first.
- Fix: `BalancedPrefix` now gets the companion's `CompanionCombatAI` component and reads its private `_targetCreature` field directly via reflection, instead of asking vanilla `BaseAI`. This should let the ammo-aware, distance-aware Balanced logic actually run during real combat for the first time.
- **Caught and fixed a bug in my own version-bump edit before it shipped**: the BepInPlugin attribute's version string is a length-prefixed byte blob (`bytearray(...)`), not free text. Going from "1.4.9" (5 chars) to "1.4.10" (6 chars) changes the string length, so a same-length byte substitution is wrong — and that's exactly what I'd done, which would have produced a garbled version string ("1.4.A", from swapping the ASCII byte for '9' with the byte for the hex digit 'A' instead of writing out two characters '1','0' and bumping the length-prefix byte). Caught by manually decoding the actual blob bytes (prefix length + string) before shipping, not by a build error — `ilasm` has no way to know the "correct" version string, so this class of mistake assembles cleanly and only shows up as a wrong string at runtime. Fixed: length prefix `05`→`06`, bytes now correctly spell `1.4.10`, decoded and reconfirmed against the final source text.
- Verified: `PrepareMethod` 355/355 ok, resources byte-identical (27 files, 11,650,347 bytes), version blob manually decoded from the assembled source to confirm `id=com.profmags.companions`, `name=Offline Companions`, `version=1.4.10` with correct trailer bytes. No new insertion points into existing game-method IL bytecode this round (only new C# helper logic + one already-simple field lookup), so the stack-balance/label-skip bug classes from 1.4.7/1.4.8 don't apply.
- Build tag `v28-balanced-real-target-fix`, version 1.4.10.
- **Lesson for future version bumps**: never assume a same-length byte swap for the BepInPlugin version blob without checking whether the digit count actually changed (single digit → double digit, e.g. 9→10, 99→100). Decode the blob (length prefix + bytes) before and after any version edit to confirm the string, the same way this was caught.

## 1.4.11 — water-safety stuck bug (fixed) + chest-overlay (diagnostic only)
User reported: (a) after opening a real chest then the companion right after, leftover misaligned/uninteractable icons stayed overlaid on the companion's panel until quitting; (b) companion got permanently stuck "just following, no reaction" after a water-chase incident, and changing stance had zero effect.

**"Stuck, no reaction" — root cause understood well enough to fix defensively.** `Character.IsSwimming()` is NOT a live position check — it's `m_swimTimer > 0.5f`, and `m_swimTimer` is reset to 0 every frame the character is genuinely at swim depth, then counts UP once out of it (vanilla `Character.UpdateWater`). The exact live-game semantics of when this clears reliably weren't something I could fully verify without running the real engine, and I didn't want to guess again given the cost of the last two wrong guesses. So the fix is defensive rather than root-cause-precise: `WaterSafety.Tick` now has a hard 25-second cap (`MaxSeekSeconds`) on how long it will keep intercepting `CompanionAI.UpdateAI` for shore-seeking, regardless of what `IsSwimming()` reports. This directly prevents the observed symptom (permanent freeze — while `Tick` returns true, `UpdateAI` returns immediately and skips ALL other logic including stance-driven combat, which is exactly why changing stance had no effect) even if the exact underlying vanilla-timer edge case isn't fully pinned down. Logs `[WaterSafety] giving up on shore-seeking after 25s (safety timeout) - resuming normal behavior.` if it ever fires, which will confirm whether this was the mechanism.

**Chest overlay — diagnostic only, not yet fixed.** Read through the existing `_savedContainerGrid`/`_savedVanillaGroup` mechanism in `CompanionInteractPanel` (used at two call sites) and confirmed it only manages the gamepad UI navigation group, not visual element visibility — consistent with an original-notes line from early in this project ("vanilla's real grid is left alone"), which may be the actual gap: nothing appears to hide vanilla's own container-grid GameObject/icons when the companion panel takes over from a real chest. Rather than guess a fix blind (cost of wrong UI guesses has been high this project), added `UiDiag.LogContainerSwitch`, called once at the top of `CompanionInteractPanel.Show()` whenever the previous open container differs from the new one. Logs the previous container's name, whether vanilla's `InventoryGui.m_containerGrid` GameObject is active (both `activeInHierarchy` and `activeSelf`), and how many of its elements are still `activeSelf` — capped at 20 total. Next repro (open a chest, then the companion) should show definitively whether vanilla's grid is still active/rendering at that point, which tells us exactly what to hide/clear.

**Bug caught before shipping, again**: my Show() diagnostic insertion initially landed after IL_0072's label instead of the label moving onto my new code — same "branch skips over new code" class as the 1.4.8 CompanionRadialMenu bugs. This time caught proactively (checked whether the insertion point was a branch target before assuming safety) rather than after building. Verified via disassembling the actual compiled DLL and confirming both `brfalse.s IL_0072` branches land on the new code's first instruction.

**Version bump lesson applied**: 1.4.10→1.4.11 is same-length ("10"→"11", both 2 digits) so a same-length byte swap was correct here — but decoded the blob before and after anyway to confirm, per the process change from last time.

Build tag `v29-water-timeout-ui-diag`, version 1.4.11. Verified: `PrepareMethod` 357/357 ok, resources byte-identical, manual bytecode branch-target trace on the one new game-method insertion (Show()), version blob decoded and confirmed `1.4.11`.

## 1.4.12 — reverted the broken Show() edit, plus found and fixed real bugs in the verification tooling itself
- 1.4.11's `UiDiag` hook into `CompanionInteractPanel.Show()` crashed the game with `InvalidProgramException` on every single attempt to open the companion's inventory (3/3 attempts in the user's log, spread across the session). This is despite that exact insertion having been checked by hand-tracing the real disassembled bytecode at the time — the hand trace was locally correct but apparently something else was wrong that extensive further investigation (see below) could not pin down before the decision was made to just revert.
- **Investigation before reverting**: built a proper from-scratch IL decoder and full data-flow stack-depth simulator using .NET's own `System.Reflection.Emit.OpCode` metadata (`StackBehaviourPop`/`Push`) instead of a hand-typed opcode table, specifically to avoid the kind of manual-tracing error that caused this in the first place. Validated it against `CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat` (confirmed working) before trusting it — found zero conflicts there, confirming the tool itself was sound for that method. Ran it against the broken `Show()` and initially found conflicts, which led to two real, separate bugs being found and fixed **in the verification tool itself**, not in the mod:
  1. `ldftn` (used for delegate/event-handler wiring, e.g. `_nameInput.onValueChanged.AddListener(...)`) carries a method token operand but does NOT invoke it — it just pushes a function pointer (Pop0/Push1). The tool was incorrectly computing its stack effect using the same "resolve method arity" logic as `call`/`callvirt`/`newobj`, producing a false stack-mismatch report.
  2. CIL `switch` instruction targets are all relative to the offset immediately *after the whole instruction* (after all N target offsets), not incrementally after each target as it's read. The tool's loop used a shifting base, corrupting every switch-case target except the last — this alone produced 29 false "conflict"/"mid-instruction" reports in `CompanionRadialMenu.ExecuteSelectedAction` (the stance-dispatch method, which the user had already confirmed works correctly), which is what proved the tool itself was still buggy rather than the mod.
  - After fixing both, re-ran the corrected tool against every game method touched this session (`Show`, `UpdateAI`, `UpdateCombat`, `CompanionRadialMenu.Show/Update/ExecuteSelectedAction`) directly against the shipped 1.4.12 DLL: **zero conflicts in all of them**, all instructions reached except the expected `finally`-block cleanup code in `UpdateAI` (unreachable by ordinary control-flow simulation since the CLR enters `finally` blocks via exception unwinding, not branches — confirmed as a simulator limitation, not a bug, by inspecting what the 4 "unreached" instructions actually were: `constrained./callvirt Dispose()/endfinally`, the standard `foreach`-enumerator-disposal pattern).
- **Despite all this, the original root cause of the Show() crash was never identified.** The decision was made to revert rather than keep chasing it, given Show() is a critical, frequently-called method and the risk of shipping a third broken build was too high. The reverted `Show()` was diffed byte-for-byte against the pre-1.4.11 source and confirmed identical.
- **Net effect**: 1.4.12 = 1.4.10's `Show()` (exact) + 1.4.11's water-safety timeout fix (kept, unaffected, separately re-verified clean) + a dead, unreferenced `UiDiag` helper class left compiled-in but never called (harmless — inert code cannot cause a runtime failure; not worth the risk of further edits to remove it right now).
- **Chest-overlay diagnostic is gone** (it was the thing that broke `Show()`) — that investigation is back to square one. Next attempt at it should use the now-validated stack-depth tool (`stacktrace.cs`, kept in the container at `/home/claude/vref/`) to verify any new insertion into `Show()` or similar methods *before* shipping, not just a manual/spot-checked trace.
- Build tag `v30-revert-show-uidiag`, version 1.4.12 (length-6 version string, decoded and confirmed: `id=com.profmags.companions`, `name=Offline Companions`, `version=1.4.12`, trailer `00 00`).

## 1.4.13 — shield policy + overhead bar values
User answers: 3+ enemies triggers the block-power (tower) shield swap. Overhead bars: HP and STAM get numbers now (weight deferred), current/max format, one toggle for the whole overhead display + one separate toggle for just the numbers, add titles directly on the bar text for colorblind accessibility, and a fade-out for each bar when full.

**Shield (`ShieldLogic.SelectShield`, called from a fully-rewritten `CompanionCombatAI.TryEquipShield`)**:
- Scores every shield in inventory two ways: `m_timedBlockBonus` (parry) and `m_blockPower` (block/tower). Picks the best of whichever category applies.
- wantBlock = health% < `HealthRetreatPct` OR nearby-enemy count >= new config `ShieldMultiAttackerCount` (default 3, range 1-10, F8 Combat category). Enemy count uses a new helper (`Character.GetAllCharacters()` + vanilla's own static `BaseAI.IsEnemy(a,b)`, 15m radius) since no such helper existed anywhere in the mod.
- No numeric hysteresis margin (unlike weapons) — a per-companion `Hashtable` just remembers which category ("parry"/"block") is currently equipped and only re-equips on an actual category change, avoiding flicker without needing a percentage margin.
- `TryEquipShield`'s entire method body was replaced (not edited in place) — old body scanned inventory manually inline in IL; new body is a ~19-instruction delegate to the C# helper. Full-body replacement was chosen deliberately after the last two sessions' partial-insertion bugs: replacing everything between a method's `{` and `}` carries none of the "existing branch/label lands on stale code" risk that bit us twice building 1.4.7/1.4.8.

**Overhead bar values (`OverheadStats.Update`, hooked into the existing `UpdateHuds_Patch.Postfix`)**:
- Key discovery: vanilla's own `EnemyHud.HudData` (a private nested struct) already HAS `m_healthText`, `m_staminaText` (both `TMPro.TextMeshProUGUI`) and a health `GuiBar` — vanilla just only populates/shows them for ridden mounts (`Sadle`-based), gated behind `m_isMount`. No new UI elements were created; `OverheadStats` finds the companion's existing `HudData` via reflection (`EnemyHud.instance`'s private `m_huds` Dictionary<Character,HudData>) and drives those same, already-correctly-positioned components directly, independent of vanilla's mount gate.
- Title is inline with the number on the same text component (e.g. "HP 47/62"), not a separate label — satisfies the colorblind-accessibility ask without any new UI. When the values toggle is off, text shows just the title ("HP").
- Fade-when-full: applied to HP and the custom Stamina bar (both bar-and-text together hidden once current >= max - 0.05, shown otherwise). Deliberately NOT applied to the Weight bar — a full weight bar is the informative state (near/at capacity), unlike full health/stamina which is the "nothing to see" state; left Weight always visible, unchanged, pending user confirmation this reading is right.
- New config: `ShowOverheadBars` (bool, default true) and `ShowOverheadBarValues` (bool, default true), both under a new "Overhead" F8 category.
- Insertion point chosen deliberately for safety: added right after the *existing* `WeightBar.SetValue()` call, before the label the loop's early-exit branches already jump to — verified this specific placement means the new code only runs on the "everything valid" fallthrough path, never on the skip-paths, without needing to move any existing label (the mistake that caused both `Show()` crashes earlier this session).

**Verification, given the last two sessions' history**: `TryEquipShield` (full replacement, so trivially low-risk) — 19/19 instructions, 0 conflicts. `UpdateHuds_Patch.Postfix` — 129/133 instructions, 0 conflicts, 4 unreached instructions confirmed as the same harmless `finally`-block enumerator-disposal pattern seen and explained in the 1.4.12 investigation. Along the way, the validated stack-depth tool (`stacktrace.cs`) correctly flagged what looked like a failure but was actually a bug in the *verification harness itself* — `GetMethod("Postfix", BindingFlags.Instance|...)` without `BindingFlags.Static` can't find a method on an `abstract sealed` (static) class, which `UpdateHuds_Patch` is. Fixed the harness, re-ran, confirmed clean. `PrepareMethod`: 393/393 ok. Resources byte-identical. Version blob decoded: `1.4.13`.
- Build tag `v31-shield-overhead-stats`, version 1.4.13.
- Open item carried forward: confirm the weight-bar fade-exclusion reasoning is correct, or change it if the user wants weight to fade too (just inverted — fade when *empty*, not full — since a near-empty weight bar is the uninformative state for that one).

## 1.4.14 — overhead bars mostly broken in 1.4.13; one confirmed root cause fixed, two still unexplained
User report after testing 1.4.13: health bar looked the same color as the stamina bar, no label/numbers visible on either, the ShowOverheadBars toggle didn't hide anything, and the stamina bar "swapped to empty" instead of fading when full.
- **Confirmed and fixed** (re-reading `CreateBar`'s own IL closely): `CreateBar` instantiates the FULL bar visual (background frame + fill) as one clone, then returns a `GuiBar` component that lives on a CHILD of that clone (just the "health_fast"-named fill portion — the background frame is a separate sibling under the same parent). `OverheadStats.ApplyOne` was calling `SetActive` on `bar.gameObject`, i.e. the fill child only — hiding just the colored fill while the background frame stayed visible. That exactly matches "swaps to empty" (the frame with no fill looks empty, not gone) and very plausibly also explains "toggle doesn't hide it" (something — the frame — is still visible either way). Fix: added `VisualRoot(GuiBar)` which walks up to `bar.transform.parent.gameObject` (the full clone) and toggles that instead. Applies to both the fade-when-full logic and the `ShowOverheadBars` toggle.
- **Not yet explained, diagnostics added instead of guessing further**: no label/numbers appearing at all, and the health-bar-same-color-as-stamina report. Neither has an obvious mechanism in the code (nothing sets `.color` anywhere in `OverheadStats`; the "no text" symptom could be `FindHudData` failing to locate the companion in vanilla's `EnemyHud.m_huds` dictionary, but there was no logging on that path before now to tell). Next log should make the remaining two symptoms diagnosable instead of guessed at.

## 1.4.15 — root cause of missing overhead text found; weight bar toggle added
1.4.14's diagnostics gave a clean, unambiguous answer: `text=NULL` on every single reading, for both HP and STAM, every frame, all session. Traced this to vanilla's own `EnemyHud.ShowHud(Character c, bool isMount)`: the whole block that does `Find("Stamina/StaminaText")` / `Find("Health/HealthText")` and assigns `HudData.m_staminaText`/`m_healthText` is gated behind `if (isMount) { ... }` — for any non-mount creature, including our companion, that code simply never runs, so those two fields stay null for the HudData's entire lifetime. Not a bug in our reflection (which was working correctly and finding real values for the bar fields, `m_healthFast` etc.) — the text components just never got looked up in the first place by vanilla for this case.
- Fix: `OverheadStats.GetTextField` now falls back to doing the *same* `Find()` call vanilla does for mounts (`"Health/HealthText"`, `"Stamina/StaminaText"`, off `HudData.m_gui`, which vanilla does populate for everyone) when the cached field comes back null. Cached per hud-GameObject-instance so the Find() only runs once. If these paths genuinely don't exist in the friendly/tamed-creature hud prefab variant, this will just also come back null — a rate-limited log (`[OverheadStats] direct Find("...") -> not found`, capped at 10) will say so explicitly either way, so next log settles it either way rather than staying a mystery.
- Weight bar now respects `ShowOverheadBars` (whole bar hidden/shown together via the same `VisualRoot` fix from 1.4.14) but deliberately has no fade-when-full and no text — per the earlier reasoning (a full weight bar is the useful state to notice, not the boring one) and confirmed unnecessary: the mod already has a full over-encumbrance speech system (`CompanionTalk.IsOverweight()`, `hc_speech_overweight`, configurable via `SpeechConfig.Overweight` lines) that announces it, so the bar's job here is secondary at best.
- `OverheadStats.Update` gained a 4th parameter (`GuiBar weightBar`); the one call site (already verified safe in 1.4.13/1.4.14, on the fallthrough path after the existing `WeightBar.SetValue()` call, before the loop's shared exit label) just pushes one more argument now — stack-balanced by construction, re-verified against the actual compiled 1.4.15 DLL anyway: 131/135 instructions, 0 conflicts, same 4 harmless unreached `finally`-block instructions as every prior check this session.
- `PrepareMethod`: 396/396 ok. Resources byte-identical. Version blob decoded: `1.4.15`.
- Build tag `v33-text-find-weight-toggle`.
- The health-bar-same-color-as-stamina report from two builds ago was not repeated in the user's follow-up message and had no clear mechanism in the code (nothing sets bar color anywhere in `OverheadStats`) - it may have been a side effect of the now-fixed empty-vs-hidden bug (1.4.14). Not chased further unless it recurs.

## 1.4.17 — diagnosed and fixed the shield "tower most of the time" report; added visibility for the Balanced bow-switch report
User's 1.4.15/16 test report: Balanced stance still wouldn't switch to a bow at range (their own guess: "maybe the range is set too high?"), and the companion used the tower shield most of the time instead of the round/parry one. Confirmed via the actual `LogOutput.log`, not guessed:
- **Shield: root-caused and fixed.** `ShieldLogic.CountNearbyEnemies` counted every hostile-faction `Character` within 15m of the companion via `BaseAI.IsEnemy(self,c)` — with zero check for whether that creature had actually noticed/engaged anyone. The config entry's own description says "Switch to the highest block-power shield once this many enemies are **attacking** at once", but the implementation only checked *proximity + hostile faction*, not engagement. With the default `ShieldMultiAttackerCount=3`, ordinary Valheim mob density (a 2-4 Greydwarf pack, a skeleton room, anything spawned in a camp) trips this constantly even when only one enemy is actually fighting the companion — this fully explains "tower most of the time" without needing tuning-by-guesswork. Fixed by additionally requiring `BaseAI.IsAlerted()` on the candidate enemy (confirmed as a real, public, parameterless vanilla method on `BaseAI` against `Game.il`) — only counts creatures that are actually alerted/engaging something, not merely standing/wandering nearby. Radius (15m) and the default threshold (3) both left unchanged, since the false-positive source was the missing engagement check, not the numbers.
- Also added `ShieldLogic`'s first-ever log line (rate-limited to 60): every time the desired shield category actually changes, logs the chosen item name, category, current HP%, the alerted-nearby count, and the thresholds being compared against — so the next play session gives real numbers to confirm the fix (or re-tune further) instead of reasoning from code alone again.
- **Balanced bow-switch: root cause still not confirmed — found and fixed a real diagnostic-visibility bug that was actively hiding the evidence.** The user's log had exactly 3 `[Balanced]` lines total for the whole session, all `skip: BaseAI type is MonsterAI, not CompanionAI` — and *zero* `handling weapon choice for this call.` lines. Traced this to `NonCompanionLog`'s tiny hardcoded budget of 3: it was shared between two very different skip reasons — a true non-companion (every hostile Humanoid in the world calls the same patched vanilla method, so this fires constantly) and an actual companion that's simply not in Balanced stance / is in hunt mode (rare, and the one case we actually need to see). Ambient monster noise was exhausting the budget of 3 within the first few frames of the session, silently swallowing every companion-specific skip reason for the rest of the play session — so the log told us nothing either way about whether Balanced logic ever even ran for the companion. Fixed by checking `BaseAI` type first: a true non-companion still goes through the tiny `NonCompanionLog` budget, but a real companion (right AI type, wrong stance/hunt-mode) now goes through the much larger `EntryLog` budget (200) alongside the other genuine diagnostic lines, including `handling weapon choice for this call.` and the `dist={0:F1} -> ...` decision line. `BowSwitchDistance`/`MeleeSwitchDistance` defaults confirmed unchanged and reasonable (20m / 12m) — not touched, since there's no log evidence yet that the numbers themselves are the problem rather than the stance/logging gap. **Needs one more test + log** to actually diagnose this one; the fix this round is purely "make sure we can see what's happening," not a guess at the underlying cause.
- No existing game-method IL was touched — both `WeaponLogic` and `ShieldLogic` were rebuilt as full-class-body replacements from the updated `Companions_helpers.cs` (the low-risk pattern established earlier this project), spliced in place of the old class bodies. `ilasm` reassembled clean with no errors. Full-runtime `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` verification could not be run this session (fresh sandbox's installed mono runtime's corlib doesn't match the game's Unity-flavored `mscorlib.dll` closely enough to load/JIT against it outside the game itself — an environment limitation, not a code issue); relying instead on (a) clean `ilasm` structural reassembly and (b) both methods being wholesale replacements generated by the C# compiler rather than hand-edited near existing branches/labels, which is the specific bug class that has caused real crashes earlier in this project.
- Build tag `v35-shield-alerted-filter-balanced-logging`, version 1.4.17 (length-6 version string, same-length byte edit, decoded and confirmed before and after: `id=com.profmags.companions`, `name=Offline Companions`, `version=1.4.17`, trailer `00 00`).
- **Important housekeeping note for next time**: partway into this session the user's uploaded `Companions_source.il`/`Companions_progress_notes.md` turned out to be a stale 1.3.9 snapshot (from long before any of the 1.4.x roadmap work), caught by decoding the version blob and finding none of the `WeaponLogic`/`ShieldLogic`/`OverheadStats`/`TorchOpener` classes present at all. The user re-uploaded the correct 1.4.16 files and work proceeded from there. Worth a quick version-blob sanity check early whenever a fresh upload arrives, given this already happened once.

### Next steps (unchanged roadmap otherwise)
- **Send a fresh test + `LogOutput.log`** after this build. Specifically want to see: (a) any `[Shield] -> ...` lines (confirms the alerted-filter fix is working and whether the tower shield now triggers only in genuinely multi-enemy fights), and (b) `[Balanced] handling weapon choice for this call.` / `dist={0:F1} -> ...` lines (finally tells us whether Balanced logic engages for the companion at all, and if so, what distance/weapon values it's actually computing — from there we'll know whether `BowSwitchDistance` needs tuning or whether there's a different bug).
