## 1.4.27 — found and fixed the real "won't swap off the bow" bug (direct orders bypassed the combat AI), plus the flee/shield fixes approved last round

**The user caught a bad diagnosis.** Last round's log analysis (from `19ce4b3f-LogOutput.log`) concluded the "companion held the bow and blocked instead of swapping to melee" symptom was caused by the flee branch in `CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat` freezing all gear decisions below 30% HP. The user pushed back: "Are you looking at the current log file? Never did fleeing happen in it." Re-checked directly against the log this round:
- **Confirmed: no flee occurred.** There is no `[Flee]`-tagged log line anywhere in the 189,000-line file (the flee branch had no logging at all — a genuine "log why, don't guess" gap), and the companion's health never actually drops in the trailing ~46,000 lines; every `[Combat] GetMaxHealth` line there is just **max HP** drifting down as food buffs expire, not damage. The flee-branch theory, as applied to that log, was wrong.
- **The real encounter:** at line 185664, the user issued a `[Direct]` attack command against a greyling. ~2,000 log-lines later there's exactly one `[Durability] Armor reduction` line (took 4.7 dmg, mostly blocked by armor), then the game quits a few seconds after. **Zero `[Ranged]`/`[EQUIPDIAG]`/`[Shield]` lines appear anywhere in that whole encounter** — the custom combat AI's weapon/shield dispatch (`SelectBestAttack`) never ran at all.

**Root cause, found by tracing the IL:** `CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat` starts with `if (!_engaged) return false;` — nothing else in the method runs until `_engaged` is true. `_engaged` is only ever set by `CompanionCombatAI.Engage(Character)`, which also resets weapon-switch cooldowns, timers, etc. `CompanionInteract.DirectAttack` (the code behind the player-issued attack command) was **never calling `Engage`** — it only poked `CompanionAI.m_targetCreature` directly and called `SetAlerted(true)`. There is a bridge in `CompanionAI.UpdateAI` that's supposed to notice a manually-set `m_targetCreature` and route it into `_combatAI.Engage(...)` — but that bridge only runs when `_combatAI.IsEngaged` is currently *false*, and only after `BaseAI.FindEnemy()` (vanilla's own detection) comes back empty. In other words: a direct-order attack was relying on a fallback path that vanilla enemy-detection or a stale "already engaged" flag could silently swallow, so the custom AI's weapon/ammo/shield/flee logic — the whole point of this mod's combat layer — could simply never engage for a player-issued attack command. Whatever the companion did in that fight (holding the bow, "blocking") was pure vanilla base-AI behavior with no ammo-fallback, no shield logic, and no flee logic in play at all.

**Fix — direct orders now use the exact same entry point as natural detection.** Added `CompanionAI.DirectEngage(Character target)`: calls `SetAlerted(true)` then unconditionally calls `_combatAI.Engage(target)` — the identical method the natural-detection path calls, so a direct order now always (re-)engages the custom combat AI regardless of whatever it was doing a moment ago, instead of depending on a field being noticed on some future tick. `DirectAttack` now calls this instead of touching `m_targetCreature`/`SetAlerted` directly. `CompanionCombatAI.Engage` now logs `[Combat] Engage -> target="..." stance=...` every time it's called (low-frequency — once per combat start, not spammy) — the same log line will now fire identically for a direct order or a naturally-detected enemy, so the next log will directly prove they're both going through the same logic, per the user's ask.

**The three approved flee/shield fixes, now built:**
1. New BepInEx config entry `FleeHealthPct` (Combat section), default **15%**, range 5%–60% — separate from `HealthRetreatPct` (still 30%, still just the shield-category switch). The flee branch in `UpdateCombat` now reads this instead of a hardcoded `0.3f` literal.
2. While fleeing, the companion now calls `TryEquipShield()` (the same shield-selection logic `SelectBestAttack` uses) before running from its target — so it can pick a defensive shield to cover its retreat. (`ShieldLogic.SelectShield` already logs every pick, so this shows up as ordinary `[Shield]` lines with no extra logging needed.)
3. "Defend if cornered, then keep trying to flee": `CompanionAI.FleeFrom`'s return value (a thin wrapper over vanilla `BaseAI.Flee`, which returns `false` when it can't find anywhere to run) is now checked instead of discarded. If flee succeeds, behavior is unchanged (skip normal combat that tick). If it fails, a new `[Flee] could not create distance (blocked/cornered) -> defending itself this tick, will keep trying to flee` line logs once and the method falls through into the normal combat flow instead of returning — so a cornered companion fights back with whatever it just equipped, and re-checks its health/flee decision fresh next tick.
4. Added `[Flee] triggered -> hp={0:P0} ... target="..."` logging at the point flee actually fires, closing the "this branch never logs anything" gap that made last round's diagnosis unverifiable in the first place.

Build tag `v45-direct-engage-fix+flee-config+flee-shield+flee-cornered-defend`, version 1.4.27 (`4.26`→`4.27`). `ilasm` reassembled clean, no errors or warnings. Verified via `monodis --typedef` (all classes present, no crash) and a UTF-16 string dump confirming the new build tag/version strings and all four new log-message strings are baked into the binary. (Targeted `monodis --filter`/`--method` dumps of the specific edited methods both crash on this file — same pre-existing large-file tool limitation as prior rounds, not new; relying on `ilasm`'s zero-error reassembly + typedef + string checks, consistent with how 1.4.26 was verified.)

### Next steps
- Test a direct-order attack (whatever the attack-command bind is) against something the companion is out of ammo for, and check the log for the new `[Combat] Engage` line firing, followed by normal `[Ranged]`/`[EQUIPDIAG]` weapon-swap lines — that confirms direct orders are now routing through the same logic as a naturally-detected enemy.
- Test getting a companion down near 15% HP in a fight it can't immediately escape and check for `[Flee] triggered`, a `[Shield]` pick right after it, and (if genuinely cornered) the new `[Flee] could not create distance` line.
- `FleeHealthPct` defaults to 15% but is configurable in the BepInEx config file if that number needs tuning after a real test.
- The `AIGateDiag`-tagged "just sits there and won't fight" report is still open from a few rounds back — no update this round either way.
- Roadmap items not yet started (unchanged): Passive-stance flee-to-player-if-following/flee-to-home-if-working; night light while foraging/gathering after dark; the 27 dead config entries; promoting `ReturnLogic`'s constants to BepInEx config entries; a possible second dedicated "return to me" keybind.
- Previously-agreed, not recently re-verified: torch opener should pick the *best* torch among all usable ones, not just the first found.

## 1.4.30 — bed claiming + no-bed complaint

User reported companions would take over a player's own personal bed, and asked for them to "claim" a bed instead - discussed two designs:
- **Real ownership** (`Bed.SetOwner`/`RPC_SetOwner`): rejected. This writes into the bed's actual networked ZDO using a synthetic non-player ID, which is real save-file/multiplayer-replicated state the vanilla game wasn't designed to hold, with unverifiable side effects and no clean "unclaim."
- **Fake/cosmetic claim** (chosen): don't touch the bed's real ownership at all. Track claims ourselves and patch only the display text.

Implementation, all in `CompanionRest` (private class, holds the bed-sleep logic) unless noted:
- `_bedClaimNames` — new `static Dictionary<int32,string>` (bed Unity instance ID -> companion display name), parallel to the pre-existing `_claimedBeds` (`HashSet<int32>`, in-memory only, resets naturally on scene reload since instance IDs aren't persisted anyway — same lifetime as our new dict, so no extra persistence work needed).
  - **Claimed** in `FinalizeSleep()` right alongside the existing `_claimedBeds.Add(...)` call, using `this._setup`'s Component name (falls back to the literal string `"Companion"` if `_setup` is somehow null).
  - **Released** in the "stopped sleeping" method (the one logging `[Rest] Stopped sleeping...`) right alongside the existing `_claimedBeds.Remove(...)` call.
- `FindNearbyBed()` — added a `bed.GetOwner() != 0` check (skip the candidate) right after the existing null-check on the found `Bed` component, before the `_claimedBeds`-based inter-companion dedup logic that was already there. This is the actual fix for "don't use someone else's bed" — a real player-owned bed (checked via the game's real `Bed.GetOwner()`) is now off-limits to companions, independent of the cosmetic dictionary above.
- `_needsBed` — new `bool` field, set `true` in `TryAutoSleep()` when `FindNearbyBed()` comes back null (i.e., it was time to sleep but no usable bed was found), `false` whenever a bed is found. Exposed via new public `NeedsBed()` getter method. Note: this flag is only updated on ticks where `TryAutoSleep` actually reaches the bed-search step (night, not in combat, not Stay-Home, scan-timer elapsed) - it does NOT get cleared on the various earlier-return paths (enemy nearby, daytime, etc.), so a companion that failed to find a bed last night may still show as "needs a bed" during the day until its next successful sleep. Minor, acceptable given how `SayContextual`'s priority chain works (higher-priority states like combat/hunger take precedence anyway).
- New Harmony patch class `BedClaimHoverTextPatch` (`[HarmonyPatch(typeof(Bed), "GetHoverText")]`, postfix) - looks up the hovered bed's instance ID in `_bedClaimNames`; if found, appends `" (claimed by <name>)"` to the vanilla hover text via `string.Format`. Purely cosmetic - reads our own dictionary, never touches `Bed`'s real fields.
- `SpeechConfig.NoBed` - new `string[]` category (5 default lines), added the same way as the existing `Hungry`/`Repair`/etc. categories. `CompanionTalk.get_NoBedLines()` mirrors the existing `get_HungryLines()`-style getters.
- `CompanionTalk` - added a cached `_rest` field (`GetComponent<CompanionRest>()` in `Awake()`, same pattern as its existing `_food`/`_setup` fields) so it can reach `CompanionRest.NeedsBed()`. In `SayContextual()`'s existing priority chain (Combat > Overweight > Hungry > NeedsRepair > ... work-mode lines), inserted a NeedsBed check right after the NeedsRepair block, before the work-mode switch - so it's a personal-state complaint at the same tier as hunger/repair, not tied to any specific work mode.

Compiled clean via `ilasm`, verified via `monodis --typedef` (new `BedClaimHoverTextPatch` class present, no crash) and `strings -e l` (new build tag, version string, and both `"{0} (claimed by {1})"` and one of the new NoBed lines confirmed baked in). Untested in-game as of delivery - next test should check: (1) a companion picks an unclaimed bed and its hover text shows "claimed by <name>" while in use, and clears after they get up; (2) a companion does NOT take a bed the player has personally claimed (hold-interact on it themselves first); (3) with zero available beds nearby, the companion eventually says a NoBed line at night.

## 1.4.31 — configurable auto-respawn timer on top of the existing manual tombstone resurrect

**User's ask, prompted by a challenge to an earlier ("resurrection is manual-only") conclusion:** "There is no respawn mechanism at all in the mod? Can you double check? ... I know I rez the NPC from their tombstone. Thats not always practical." Investigation found there *is* a full automatic-respawn subsystem in `CompanionManager` (`_respawnQueue`/`QueueRespawn`/`ProcessRespawns`/`DoRespawn`) — but it's never called anywhere. It's genuinely dead code, entirely superseded (not broken) by the active manual system, `CompanionTombstoneResurrect` (hold-interact on the tombstone). User chose **Option 2**: keep the manual system as-is and add a configurable timer on top of it that reuses the same tested resurrect logic, rather than reviving the old dead subsystem.

**Design, worked out with the user turn by turn (each point explicitly confirmed before building):**
- Respawn location: the companion's own claimed bed if it has one; otherwise world spawn, or optionally near the owner instead (config toggle, off by default). Manual tombstone resurrect is unchanged — always at the tombstone.
- Inventory: kept 100% by default now (a behavior change from the old always-drop-everything). Two new toggles: drop everything into the tombstone instead, and (sub-option) drop everything except currently-equipped gear.
- Tombstone lifecycle: if nothing was dropped (or it's empty after recovery), it's destroyed automatically — reusing logic `DoResurrect()` already had. If something was dropped, the tombstone stays as an ordinary lootable container and the companion will walk over and loot it themselves once back in range — capped at a configurable distance (default 50) specifically so they don't wander far enough to risk an unload; this only gates the decision to *commit* to going after it, not the walk itself once committed.
- No multi-tombstone tracking: confirmed with the user this should track only the single most-recent tombstone, matching how vanilla Valheim itself only meaningfully tracks a player's latest death marker (a player CAN have multiple tombstones, but the mod deliberately doesn't try to reconcile that for companions).
- Map pin: confirmed the tombstone already gets a normal minimap death-pin (pre-existing `Minimap.AddPin(..., PinType.Death, ...)` in `CreateCompanionTombstone`) — this extends "for free" to the new auto-respawn path, no separate work needed.
- Mid-implementation correction from the user: when inventory is kept (nothing left in the tombstone), the tombstone should visibly break/disappear rather than sit there as an empty container. Turned out `DoResurrect()` already had exactly this destroy-if-empty-else-keep logic from its dead-code days — reused rather than rebuilt.

**Implementation, low-risk because it reuses existing, already-tested logic wherever possible:**
- 6 new `ModConfig` entries (section "Respawn"): `AutoRespawnEnabled` (bool, default true), `AutoRespawnDelaySeconds` (float, default 300, range 30–3600), `AutoRespawnNearOwner` (bool, default false), `DropInventoryOnDeath` (bool, default false), `DropEquippedGearOnDeath` (bool, default false), `TombstoneRecoveryRange` (float, default 50, range 5–100).
- `CompanionRest.FinalizeSleep()` now persists the claimed bed's position onto the companion's own ZDO the moment a bed claim is committed (new `HomeBedPosHash`/`HasHomeBedHash`), because `_bedTarget` itself is transient and gets cleared as soon as a sleep session ends — reading it later at time-of-auto-respawn would almost always find nothing. Same pattern the mod already uses for `HomePos`.
- `CompanionSetup.OnCompanionDeath()`/`CreateCompanionTombstone(...)` now read that bed-claim data off the dying companion's ZDO and thread it through to `CompanionTombstoneResurrect.WriteRespawnData(...)`, alongside a new keep/drop inventory split: "keep" items are pulled into a temp `Inventory`, serialized via the game's own `Inventory.Save`+`ZPackage.GetBase64()` (not hand-rolled), and only the "drop" remainder is left for the pre-existing grave-transfer logic to see — so the old drop-to-grave code needed no changes at all.
- `CompanionTombstoneResurrect.DoResurrect()` → `DoResurrect(bool isAutoRespawn)`: manual calls pass `false` (unchanged tombstone-position behavior); the new auto-respawn timer in `Update()` calls it with `true`, which branches to bed/near-owner/world-spawn positioning, restores the serialized "keep" inventory via `Inventory.Load`, and — reusing the pre-existing "items remained after pullback" check — either destroys the tombstone or (when kept) writes this tombstone's ID onto the newly-spawned companion's ZDO so the dormant `CompanionAI.UpdateTombstoneRecovery` scan-and-loot logic picks it up.
- `CompanionAI.UpdateTombstoneRecovery(float dt)` — added the distance gate discussed above: a tombstone candidate farther than `TombstoneRecoveryRange` is skipped rather than committed to.
- `CompanionManager.GetWorldSpawnPoint()`/`FindSpawnPosition(...)` changed from `private static` to `assembly static` so `CompanionTombstoneResurrect` could call them directly instead of duplicating them.

Build tag `v49-auto-respawn-and-keep-inventory`, version 1.4.31 (`4.30`→`4.31`). Every edit reassembled clean via `ilasm` on the first attempt; final build (`Companions_1431.dll`) also reassembled clean. Verified via `monodis --typedef` (clean, no crash) and `strings -e l` (confirmed all 6 new config keys, the build tag, the version string, and the `[Resurrect] Restored kept inventory` log line are baked in). A full `monodis --method` dump aborted partway through with the same pre-existing large-file crash (`dump.c:604` assertion, exit 134) seen in earlier rounds — not new, not tied to this edit; the clean `ilasm` reassembly (which validates every method, including the changed ones) plus the `--typedef`/`strings` checks are the standing verification method for this DLL. Untested in-game as of delivery.

### Hotfix (same version, build tag `v49b-auto-respawn-maxstack-fix`) — actual in-game crash found and fixed

First real in-game test surfaced two bugs from the log the user attached (`e45515dc-LogOutput.log`):

1. **The real bug, and the one causing the reported symptom** ("NPC did not become a tombstone and is stuck in place at 0 HP"): `InvalidProgramException: Invalid IL code in Companions.CompanionSetup:OnCompanionDeath (): IL_02d4: ldloc.s 26`, thrown from `Character.CheckDeath()` the instant a companion's `OnDeath` event fires — i.e. before `CreateCompanionTombstone` ever runs. Root cause: `OnCompanionDeath()`'s `CreateCompanionTombstone(...)` call site was extended this version with 2 new trailing args (`hasHomeBed`, `homeBedPos`), pushing `this` + 20 arguments onto the stack simultaneously (21 slots) right before the call — but the method's `.maxstack` directive was left at its old value, `19`. The verifier correctly rejected the method as invalid IL (not just "unverifiable" — genuinely malformed, since the declared max stack depth is smaller than what the code actually needs), so the whole death handler bailed out with an exception every single time, after already having set `_dead = true` — hence "no tombstone, but stuck in place at 0 HP forever." Fix: `.maxstack` raised `19` → `21`. (Every earlier `ilasm` reassembly passed because `ilasm` doesn't statically verify stack depth against `.maxstack` at assembly time the way the CLR verifier does at load time — this only ever surfaces at runtime, which is why it slipped through the project's usual `ilasm`-clean + `monodis --typedef` + `strings` verification and needed an actual in-game test to catch.)
2. **A separate, non-fatal bug**, spamming the log every time a player looked at a bed: `FieldAccessException: Field 'Companions.CompanionRest:_bedClaimNames' is inaccessible from method 'Companions.BedClaimHoverTextPatch:Postfix'` — a leftover from 1.4.30. `_bedClaimNames` was declared `private static` on `CompanionRest`, but `BedClaimHoverTextPatch` (a different class) reads it directly in its Harmony postfix to build the "(claimed by ...)" hover text. Fix: changed the field's accessibility from `private static initonly` to `assembly static initonly` (internal-equivalent), matching the pattern already used for cross-class hash fields elsewhere in the mod. Harmony/the hover-text postfix silently no-ops on this exception rather than crashing anything, so this bug was cosmetic (no "claimed by" text ever showed) rather than the cause of the reported symptom.

Both fixes verified via clean `ilasm` reassembly (zero errors) and `monodis --typedef`/`strings -e l` (new build tag `v49b-auto-respawn-maxstack-fix` confirmed present). Not yet re-tested in-game as of this delivery — next test is the same death/tombstone/respawn loop that originally surfaced the crash.

### Next steps
- Test the full loop: let a companion die with default settings (keep inventory, auto-respawn on), confirm it respawns at its claimed bed (or world spawn if it never claimed one) after ~5 minutes with all its items, and that the tombstone is gone.
- Test `DropInventoryOnDeath` on: confirm items land in the tombstone, the tombstone stays as a lootable container with a death-pin on the map, and the respawned companion walks over and loots it once within 50m — and does NOT commit to the walk from farther away.
- Test `DropEquippedGearOnDeath` sub-option: confirm only equipped gear survives into the new companion's inventory and everything else lands in the tombstone.
- Test manual tombstone hold-resurrect still works unchanged and cancels/no-ops the auto-respawn timer for that companion.
- Test `AutoRespawnNearOwner`: confirm it only applies when the companion has no claimed bed.
- Everything from 1.4.30's "Untested in-game" bed-claim checks is also still open.

## 1.4.32 — persistent, player-like bed claims (replaces 1.4.30's transient "occupied right now" model)

**User's ask, after 1.4.31's tombstone/respawn work shipped:** a companion should claim a bed like a player would — a standing assignment, not something that only exists while they're actually lying in it. Specifically: claim automatically if one's available and they don't have one; claim near wherever Home Position gets set (and re-claim if it moves); notice if their owner has personally settled into a bed nearby and get one of their own too, "like a real player would"; and pointing at a bed + commanding stays available as a manual override/fallback for assigning or updating a specific bed. Corrected mid-discussion: the mod already "fakes" bed ownership display rather than touching the game's real `Bed.SetOwner` (established back in 1.4.30, for good reason — real ownership is real networked save state we don't want to touch) — the ask here is for that fake claim to be **permanent-until-reassigned**, not toggle on and off with sleep the way 1.4.30 had it.

**Design confirmed with the user, piece by piece:**
- A claim is now a standing assignment (tracked on the companion's own ZDO, same spirit as Home Position), not "currently occupied." The hover text ("(claimed by \<name\>)") now reflects this permanently, not just while asleep.
- Automatic claim triggers: (1) `SetHomePosition` — immediate search within Home Zone Radius of the new point; (2) a recurring ~30s background check for a home-based companion with no valid claim; (3) noticing a real player's own claimed bed within ~20m when the companion has no claim of their own yet — confirmed this should grab a bed, not also silently set Home Position (that stays an explicit, separate action).
- Follow-only companions with no Home Position simply don't auto-claim via (1)/(2) — only via (3), the owner-bed-proximity trigger — until the player either Sets Home for them or manually assigns a bed via point-command.
- Claiming a new bed always releases whatever the companion had claimed before, freeing it for someone else.
- Point-command on a bed is the manual override: unclaimed → claim + sleep; already theirs → sleep; someone else's (real player, or a different companion) → refuse and speak a new "that's not my bed" line, don't walk over.
- Confirmed zone-loading has no bearing on any of this: Valheim's `ZoneSystem` loads/unloads per distance-from-any-player, not per base, and nothing (physics scans, `Update()` ticks) runs for a companion whose zone is unloaded — so the 30s check and owner-bed check simply don't fire while unloaded, and resume cold on next load with no drift to account for.

**Implementation** (`CompanionRest` unless noted):
- New field `_claimedBed` (the standing claim, `Bed` reference) alongside the existing `_claimedBeds`/`_bedClaimNames` static dictionaries (now genuinely persistent claims, not released each morning) and new `_bedClaimScanTimer` (float, 30s cadence).
- `FindNearbyBed` kept as the original 20m-around-current-position search (still used nowhere new); added `FindAnyBedNear(Vector3 anchor, float radius)` (nearest bed regardless of owner — used to relink a claim by stored position after a zone reload), `FindUnclaimedBedNear(Vector3, float)` (nearest bed that isn't a real player's and isn't already claimed by someone else — the one actual auto-claim searches use), and `HasOwnerBedNearby(Vector3, float)` (true if any real player has a claimed bed in range — used for the owner-proximity trigger; deliberately checks "a real player's bed", not specifically the companion's own registered owner's bed, since `Bed.GetOwner()` alone doesn't require resolving which specific player owns this companion — a reasonable simplification worth knowing about for a busy multiplayer server with several owners based near each other).
- `ClaimBed(Bed)` — releases the current claim (`ReleaseClaimedBed()`), sets `_claimedBed`, adds to `_claimedBeds`/`_bedClaimNames`, and writes `HomeBedPosHash`/`HasHomeBedHash` onto the companion's own ZDO (the same fields 1.4.31 added for auto-respawn — now written at claim time, not only after they finish a sleep cycle, so a claimed-but-not-yet-slept-in bed is still a valid respawn point).
- `ReleaseClaimedBed()` — removes the current claim from both dictionaries and clears `_claimedBed`.
- `TryClaimBedIfNeeded()` (public, called cross-class from `CompanionSetup.SetHomePosition` and from `CompanionRest.Update`'s new 30s timer) — if the current claim was taken over by a real player, releases it; otherwise, if `HasHomeBedHash` is set but nothing untoward happened, tries to relink to the actual `Bed` object at the stored position (handles the case where the claim survived a zone reload but the in-memory `_claimedBed` reference didn't); if there's still no live claim, searches near Home Position (if set) or, if not, only proceeds when `HasOwnerBedNearby` says the owner has settled in nearby — then claims the nearest unclaimed bed found.
- `CompanionSetup.SetHomePosition` — after writing the ZDO fields (unchanged), now also calls `GetComponent<CompanionRest>().TryClaimBedIfNeeded()`.
- `CompanionRest.Update` — added the 30s timer tick calling `TryClaimBedIfNeeded()`, alongside the existing per-second dispatch.
- `TryAutoSleep()` — no longer calls `FindNearbyBed()` for "any unclaimed bed nearby." Now: if there's no live claim, tries `TryClaimBedIfNeeded()` once; if still no claim, sets `_needsBed = true` and returns (the 1.4.30 "no bed" complaint fires, as intended — they will not wander to whatever's nearby); if there is a claim, sleeps in it via the existing `BeginSleep(bed, isAuto: true)`.
- `CompanionRest.DirectSleep(Bed)` changed from `void` to `bool` (accepted/refused): null bed → true (no-op, not a refusal); a real player's bed (`GetOwner() != 0`) → false; already this companion's `_claimedBed` → proceed; claimed by someone else (in `_claimedBeds` but not this companion's) → false; otherwise unclaimed → `ClaimBed(bed)` then proceed. `CompanionInteract.DirectSleep` (the point-command dispatcher) now checks this return value and, on `false`, has the companion speak a `get_NotMyBedLines()` line instead of walking over.
- New localized speech category `hc_cmd_notmybed_1/2/3` ("That's not my bed." / "That one's already taken." / "That's not mine to use."), registered the same way as the existing `hc_cmd_wake_*`/`hc_cmd_sleep_*` lines, with a matching `CompanionInteract.get_NotMyBedLines()` getter.
- `StopSleeping()` — the old "release the claim every time they get up" code (`_claimedBeds.Remove`/`_bedClaimNames.Remove`, alongside the `[Rest] Stopped sleeping...` log line) was removed outright, with a comment explaining why: claims are persistent now and only release when a different bed gets claimed or a real player takes the bed over, not just because morning came.
- `FinalizeSleep()`'s original claim-on-arrival code (from 1.4.30) was left in place rather than removed — it's now redundant with `ClaimBed` (which already ran before the companion ever started walking there) but harmless, since re-adding to a `HashSet`/re-setting the same `Dictionary` key is a no-op.

**Verification, upgraded after 1.4.31's maxstack bug slipped through the usual checks:** this round, every new/modified method's `.maxstack` was manually traced by hand (the same technique that caught the 1.4.31 bug — counting the live stack depth at the worst point, usually a call with many arguments) rather than trusting a clean `ilasm` reassembly alone, since `ilasm` does not itself verify declared `.maxstack` against actual required depth. On top of that, built a small standalone verification harness (`verify_all.cs`, using `System.Runtime.CompilerServices.RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` via reflection against all the game's real reference assemblies) that force-JITs every method in the assembly and reports any failure — confirmed via a deliberate reintroduction of the exact 1.4.31 maxstack bug that this harness does *not* reliably catch that specific class of bug under Mono's normal (non-dynamic-method) JIT path, which doesn't always enforce full stack verification for a directly-loaded assembly the way it does for Harmony's dynamic-method-based patches — so it's a useful smoke test (ran clean, 1553/1553 methods) but not a substitute for the manual maxstack trace, which remains the real check for this specific bug class. `monodis --typedef` and `strings -e l` both confirm as usual (new build tag, version, and all new strings present). Untested in-game as of delivery.

### Next steps
- Test the four claim triggers: Set Home near an available bed; the ~30s idle-with-no-claim check; noticing the owner's own claimed bed nearby (Follow companion, no Home Position); and point-command claiming a specific bed.
- Test the refusal path: point-command a companion onto a bed the player is personally using, and onto a bed a different companion already claimed — confirm the "not my bed" line fires and they don't walk over.
- Test reassignment: change Home Position to a new location with a different available bed nearby, confirm the old bed's hover text clears (no longer "claimed by") and the new one picks it up.
- Test that a companion with no claim and no nearby owner bed correctly just says the existing "no bed" line at night instead of grabbing whatever's closest.
- Confirm the claimed bed still serves as the 1.4.31 auto-respawn point even when the companion claimed it but never actually got a chance to sleep in it yet.

## 1.4.29 — game-patch API compatibility fix

Hexium's compatibility checker flagged the published 1.4.28 listing as broken against the current game build (Steam app 896660, build 25527701, refs 77e4db6efc378469) with 11 findings, all "method no longer exists":
- `Inventory.AddItem(string, int32, int32, int32, int64, string, bool)->ItemDrop+ItemData` — 3 call sites (CookController and HomesteadController crafting-into-inventory paths).
- `Piece.SetCreator(int64)` — 1 call site (FarmController placing a plant/piece).
- `VisEquipment.SetRightItem/SetLeftItem/SetChestItem/SetLegItem/SetHelmetItem/SetShoulderItem/SetUtilityItem` — all 7, in `CompanionPanel::ApplyItemToVis`.

The user supplied a fresh `Managed.zip` from their game install (dated Sep 25, the day of the patch) to confirm the actual current signatures rather than guessing from the checker's text alone:
- `AddItem` gained a trailing `[opt] bool pickedUp` param (now 8 params total) — fixed by pushing `ldc.i4.0` (false) before the call at all 3 sites, matching prior behavior (these are all "craft a fresh item into inventory" cases, not world pickups).
- `SetCreator` gained a second `valuetype [Splatform]Splatform.PlatformUserID` param — fixed by adding a new local of that type, `initobj`-ing it to a default/empty value, and passing it. Companions aren't real platform accounts, and this only affects piece-attribution metadata, not gameplay.
- The `VisEquipment.Set*Item` methods switched from `string` to `int32 itemHash` — fixed by inserting a call to the already-used `class [assembly_utils]StringExtensionMethods::GetStableHashCode(string)` helper (same pattern the mod already used for `SetHairItem`/`SetBeardItem`) before each call, and updating each callsite's target signature. `SetLeftItem` and `SetShoulderItem` also gained a `variant` param in the new signature (making them 3-arg instead of 2-arg) — passed `0` for it, matching the `quality` value those calls already passed before.

Verified via clean `ilasm` reassembly, `monodis --typedef` (no crash), and `strings -e l` confirming the new build tag/version strings are baked in. Untested in-game as of delivery — next test should re-check normal companion crafting (cook/homestead), plant/piece placement, and gear swap visuals (the radial-wheel inventory panel) still work correctly.

## 1.4.28 — fixed the actual "stays at bow range while wielding melee" bug (a movement/kiting issue, separate from the weapon-equip fix in 1.4.27)

**1.4.27's fix worked as intended.** The new `446ea847-LogOutput.log` confirms it directly: `[Combat] Engage -> target="$enemy_greydwarf" ... ` now fires for `[Direct]` attack-commanded targets, same as naturally-detected ones. The ammo-fallback logic itself is also working correctly — `[Ranged] FindReadyBow -> none ... falling back to melee` fires, `TryForceMeleeWeapon` equips the axe, and `TryEquipShield` equips a shield right after, all logged normally. So the *weapon* really is switching to the axe during the fallback. Good.

**But the user reported a different, still-real symptom:** "When the NPC falls back to melee they still try and stay at bow range." Traced this to `CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat`'s attack-range/positioning calculation (the `isRanged` flag that later drives how much distance the AI tries to keep from its target). The logic was:
```
if weapon == null:                    isRanged = (_stance == 2)   // stance fallback
elif weapon.aiAttackRange > 10:       isRanged = true
elif weapon.itemType == Bow:          isRanged = true
else (weapon present, clearly melee): isRanged = (_stance == 2)   // BUG: same stance fallback
```
The last branch was the bug: whenever the currently-equipped weapon was present but neither long-range nor a bow (i.e. unambiguously melee, like the axe during an ammo-fallback), the code still fell back to checking the companion's **configured** combat stance (`_stance`) instead of just concluding "this is melee." Since the companion's configured stance stays `2` (Ranged) the whole time — swapping to the axe is a *temporary tactical fallback*, not a stance change — `isRanged` came out `true` even while holding the axe. Downstream, `isRanged` drives the attack range used for positioning (forces a `30` fallback range instead of the weapon's real short melee range), which is exactly "stand off at bow range" behavior.

**Fix:** the stance-based fallback now only applies when there's genuinely no weapon equipped (a real transient state, e.g. mid-swap). Any time a weapon **is** equipped and it isn't long-range or a bow, `isRanged` is now unconditionally `false` — the actual equipped weapon decides ranged-vs-melee positioning, never the configured stance. This is a small, surgical branch fix in `UpdateCombat`, no new config or logging added (this runs every combat tick, so a log line here would spam — the fix is provable in-game by watching the companion actually close distance, rather than in the log).

Build tag `v46-melee-fallback-range-fix`, version 1.4.28 (`4.27`→`4.28`). `ilasm` reassembled clean, no errors or warnings; `monodis --typedef` shows all classes present with no crash; version/build strings confirmed via UTF-16 string dump.

### Next steps
- Test: run the companion out of arrows against something, and confirm it now closes to melee range with the axe instead of hovering at bow distance. `[Ranged]`/`[EQUIPDIAG]` logs already confirm the weapon swap is correct — this round is purely about whether the companion's *movement* now matches.
- Everything else from 1.4.27's next-steps list is still open/unchanged (flee-threshold test, `AIGateDiag` "just sits there" report, roadmap items).
